The **3 unclassified** symbols (`luaL_loadbuffer`/`luaL_loadstring`/`luaL_gsub`) stay null (unclassified, classified before the static Lua is dropped). Coexists with the static-linked Lua at this point (vendor/lua/*.c is dropped when the static Lua is dropped), so a live `lua_State` exists to call into; the dual-Lua sentinel-zero-read check stays silent (no new sentinel — every stub uses the live VM's own structures, no kcdx static-const sentinel). |
| Channels | Engine self-test (`src/lua_shim_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-52..66. `g_api()` is an engine-internal symbol, not a plugin export, so it self-reports from engine code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult`. Single-surface at this layer (the shim is engine-internal VM plumbing; the plugin-facing `kcdxLuaApi` rewire onto this shim lands when the static Lua is dropped, with its own parity coverage then). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/lua_shim.{h,cpp}` + `lua_shim_stubs.cpp` ship the forward + stub layers + `Resolve()`; the keystone (P3 step 2) now calls `Resolve()` at engine init (worker, in `BuildAndAdoptVM`), so `g_api()` is populated and the shim is exercised end-to-end against the adopted VM. Live-confirmed 2026-06-05. |
| Test plugin | none — engine self-test (`src/lua_shim_selftest.cpp`, `RunSelfTestOnce()` one-shot from the per-tick self-report block right after cap-66). Calls into the live `lua_State` via `kcdx::hooks::CurrentLuaState()`. |
| Auto-pass check | `in-game` (the live VM is captured at the first `lua_pcall`, reliably non-null by the main menu; the self-test retries each tick until then, never a false FAIL). Five falsifiable rows: **CAP-79-roundtrip** — pushes `"kcdx-shim-roundtrip-42"` through forwarded `lua_pushlstring` (id 30), reads back via `lua_tolstring` (id 39) + `lua_type` (id 28), asserts type==LUA_TSTRING + len + bytes; FAILS on mismatch or null member. **CAP-79-internal-gated** — `IsInternalOnly` true for lua_newstate/lua_close/lua_setallocf/lua_atpanic, false for lua_pushlstring/lua_pcall/lua_settop; FAILS on a tautology gate. **CAP-79-stubs** (stub layer) — exercises stub classes through `g_api`: `lua_pushnil`→type LUA_TNIL; `lua_pushboolean(1)`→`lua_toboolean` true; `lua_pushinteger(42)`→type LUA_TNUMBER + `lua_tointeger`==42; `lua_gettop` counts +3 from the entry depth (the gettop-stub arithmetic); `luaL_register` installs a global table read back via `lua_getfield` (the luaI_openlib-reimplementation stub); restores the stack with `lua_settop(entryTop)`. FAILS if any value reads back wrong, gettop miscounts, or the table is absent. **CAP-79-gc-barrier** (stub layer) — asserts `GcBarrierBacked()` (luaC_barrierf, id 127, resolved non-null); the GC-barrier-safety invariant. FAILS if the barrier primitive did not resolve. **CAP-79-layout** (stub layer) — `ValidateLayout(L)`: the mainthread self-pointer invariant `G(L)->mainthread`(g+0xB0)==L; FAILS if a game update shifted the struct layout the stubs read. No player input. |
| Last result | ✅ PASS (live 2026-06-05, `kcdx-dev_2026-06-05_17-28-46.log`, boot + save-load) — all 5 rows green once the keystone wired `Resolve()` at init: roundtrip ("kcdx-shim-roundtrip-42" pushed + read back identical via the shim, LUA_TSTRING/len/bytes), internal-gated (IsInternalOnly discriminates the 4 lifecycle fns from the 3 plugin-facing), stubs (pushnil→nil, pushboolean→true, pushinteger(42)→TNUMBER+42, gettop +3 from entry depth 7, luaL_register installed a readable global table), gc-barrier (luaC_barrierf id 127 backed), layout (mainthread self-pointer holds on the live VM). The pinned contract is now LIVE. |
| Notes | The required-miss BAIL path of `Resolve()` (forward + `BindStubPrimitives`) cannot be exercised as a live row without denying the running game its Lua VM; its falsifiability is the structured `LOG_ERROR_KV` + `return false` in the `FORWARD` macro and `BindStubPrimitives` (fail loud / never swallow a miss and return success), verified by code review. CAP-79-internal-gated / -stubs / -layout are the live negatives that CAN go red. **GC-barrier coverage limitation (surfaced):** no seam stub writes a GC pointer into a black GC object — the verified push bodies (`lua_pushthread` incl.) write only STACK slots, which need no barrier; the barrier-callers (`lua_replace`/`lua_setupvalue`/`lua_setmetatable`) are RESOLVED forwarders, not stubs. So CAP-79-gc-barrier asserts barrier AVAILABILITY (resolved non-null), not a live barrier CALL — a live call is not constructible from a self-test at this layer. **Seed reality:** the design's "93 resolved / ~24 stubs" headline does NOT match the three-file seed — the actual split is **90 forwarded + 31 stubbed + 3 unclassified**. The 3 (`luaL_loadbuffer`/`luaL_loadstring`/`luaL_gsub`) are UNCLASSIFIED — tracked as unclassified pending an RE classification pass that settles each before the static Lua is dropped. **`lua_newthread` + `lua_cpcall` not-usable (surfaced):** the RE analysis gives no reimplementation strategy for either — `lua_newthread` allocates via the unseeded `luaE_newthread`; `lua_cpcall` needs the static `f_Ccall`/`savestack` frame internals. Their stubs fail LOUD (`lua_newthread`→NULL, `lua_cpcall`→LUA_ERRRUN) rather than fabricate a body (a guessed body would corrupt the VM — verified against the binary, not guessed from the prologue). CryEngine inlines/open-codes both at its sites (own WHGame copies), so engine use does not need them; a plugin needing them is a surfaced gap, not a silent wrong call. Rows evolve at later layers (the init/VM-build layer calls `Resolve()` at init + builds the VM; the static-Lua-drop layer rewires `kcdxLuaApi` onto this shim with parity coverage AND drops `vendor/lua/*.c`; that layer's gate — the dual-Lua sentinel-zero-read check silent across a full save-load — is the structural cure). |

## CAP-81: keystone — kcdx builds the one Lua VM, the engine ADOPTS it (no force-load)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The KEYSTONE of the kcdx-owns-the-VM mechanism (Phase 11 P3 step 2). On the worker thread (after WHGame is mapped + refdb open + `hooks::Install()`), `lua_vm_build::BuildAndAdoptVM()` (`src/lua_vm_build.{h,cpp}`) resolves the Lua symbol shim (`lua_shim::Resolve`), builds the ONE `lua_State` via the shim's `lua_newstate` (WHGame's compiled Lua body — **no force-load**, the worker waits for the game to map WHGame), validates the mainthread self-pointer invariant `[L->l_G + 0xB0] == L`, PUBLISHES it into `hooks::g_L` with a RELEASE edge, and installs the **`lua_newstate`-callee intercept** — an engine-stamped `Mode::Replace` chain entry (`hook_chain::AddCEngine`, `engine.lua_newstate`) so the engine's `CScriptSystem::Init` (game main thread, ~2s later) calls `lua_newstate`, gets kcdx's pre-built state back (the original never runs — no second VM), and runs its own `storedebug=0`/`luaL_openlibs`/3-registrar sequence ON kcdx's state. The cross-thread handoff is a release/acquire happens-before edge, never timed (`concurrency.md`). The `engine.lua_pcall` hook's `g_L` store became a guarded CONFIRMATION (`HookedLuaPcall_Engine`): on the keystone path it asserts the engine's incoming L equals the published L and fails LOUD on a mismatch (a divergent L = a silent second VM); on a fallback path (build failed) it captures L as before. Coexists with the static-linked Lua this step (the drop is P5) — this state is WHGame's, so it introduces no new sentinel. |
| Channels | Engine self-test (`src/cap81_vm_adopt_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-79 / cap-80 / cap-66. The VM build + adoption is engine-internal boot plumbing (`lua_vm_build::BuiltState()` / the engine's `lua_newstate` intercept are not plugin-observable), so it self-reports from engine code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult`. Single-surface (engine-internal VM/boot plumbing). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/lua_vm_build.{h,cpp}` ships the worker VM build + the `lua_newstate` Replace intercept; `dllmain.cpp` calls `BuildAndAdoptVM()` right after `EngineHooksInstalled`; `hooks.cpp` ships the `PublishLuaState` authoritative writer + the guarded-confirm. Live-confirmed 2026-06-05: `engine_adopted_kcdx_state` fired, built==live==0x1616D3B0990, no second VM, no FAULTED. |
| Test plugin | none — engine self-test (`src/cap81_vm_adopt_selftest.cpp`, `RunSelfTestOnce()` one-shot from the per-tick self-report block right after cap-79). Reads the live adopted state via `kcdx::hooks::CurrentLuaState()` and the kcdx-built state via `lua_vm_build::BuiltState()`. |
| Auto-pass check | `in-game` (the live adopted state is captured at the first `lua_pcall`; the intercept fires at `CScriptSystem::Init` — the self-test retries each tick until BOTH have landed, never a false FAIL). Four falsifiable rows: **CAP-81-single-state** — `BuiltState()` non-null AND `InterceptFired()` true AND `CurrentLuaState() == BuiltState()` (the engine's live `lua_pcall` runs on the SAME state kcdx built — ONE VM); FAILS if the build/publish failed, the intercept never fired, or the live state differs (a second VM — the dual-Lua hazard, with the `MID_HOOK lua_pcall.divergent_L` ERROR). THE single-state assertion. **CAP-81-mainthread** — `ValidateLayout(live)`: `G(L)->mainthread`(g+0xB0)==L on the ADOPTED state; FAILS on a struct-layout shift. **CAP-81-kcdx-tables** — `_G.kcdx` is a table with a populated `kcdx.log` sub-table on the adopted state; FAILS if the kcdx surface never registered on it. **CAP-81-cryengine-script** — `_G.System` is a table on the adopted state (CryEngine ran `luaL_openlibs` + its registrars ON kcdx's state); FAILS if CryEngine's scripts never bound. No player input. |

The engine-stamped C-kind Replace entry runs synchronously on the game main thread (the `isEngine` C-kind off-thread carve-out in `DispatchPre`) and its return becomes `lua_newstate`'s result; `runOriginal=false` so the original never allocates a second VM. `lua_newstate` is resolved BY NAME (refdb id 114, AP1). **No `CScriptSystem::Init`-itself hook** (the P1-falsified fallback was not built). **Table-registration thread (settled by PROBE FIXC, not reasoned):** `kcdx.*` registration stays on the EXISTING game-thread first-tick path (`HookedUpdate` → `RegisterKcdxTable`), now running on the worker-built adopted state — satisfying CAP-81-kcdx-tables. The registration thread is therefore IMMATERIAL to safety; the game-thread path is kept (design §6.4 corrected to match). The full shim-routing of the binder is the static-Lua-drop step's concern. **Fallback preserved:** a `BuildAndAdoptVM()` failure is fail-loud-and-continue — the engine builds its own VM, the static-Lua bootstrap still binds, the `lua_pcall` guard flags any divergence (no fallback removed in the keystone step). PROBE Q stays silent (no new sentinel). cap-80 is the highest prior cap row; cap-81 is the next free id. |

It probes `function_entry` FIRST to distinguish "statement data absent" (degraded) from "a specific locator missed" (a real FAIL). **Ground truth verified** against `data/reference-dev.sqlite` (SaveGame av_id 304855, 59 statements; idx 0/58/8/13/58; brl 3/30/5; captures param_7 stack/8 + puVar6 register/8) — the expected values are the curated DB's, never adjusted to a resolution result. **Row id:** the brief named `cap-79`, but cap-79 (Lua shim) / cap-80 (early-hook) / cap-81 (VM-adopt) are LIVE and cap-82 is claimed by the parallel Phase-11 P4 work — `cap-83` is the next free id per the `test-suite.md` convention. **Deploy note:** the deployed `reference.sqlite` currently lacks the `statements`/`referenced_vars` tables — the live PASS path requires the deploy to regenerate the USER DB with the statement tables (else the self-test reports DEGRADED PASS, by design). |

On the FIRST callback fire (one-shot guarded) the plugin reports PASS. `kcdxMessage_InputLoaded` backstop reports loud FAIL if the callback never fires by then (`lua_pcall` is called continuously from plugin load onward — a missed fire by `InputLoaded` means the chain install never wired the dispatch path). FALSIFIABLE: zero handle → loud FAIL; missed fire by backstop window → loud FAIL with the handle state captured (`IsApplied` / `GetReason`). No player input. |

| Notes | The C++ regression net for the engine-direct migration — pairs with CAP-59 (Lua peer, the FAIL row that motivated the migration). Both rows assert the install + first-fire contract; together they prove the chain machinery routes a plugin install at a target the engine itself hooks. The other 5 migration sites (`engine.frealloc_canary`, `engine.modmanager_ctor`, `engine.bugsplat_ctor`, `engine.savegame`, `engine.loadgame`) have their own follow-up rows pending — each is one migration cycle routing the site through the engine hook chain. |

## CAP-70: `kcdx_scan <module> "<pattern>"` — the engine-owned `~`-console AOB-discovery command

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The engine-owned `kcdx_scan` `~`-console command (`src/console_commands_scan.cpp`, registered unconditionally at `console::Init`): an author types `kcdx_scan <module> "<AOB pattern>"` to iterate an AOB in-game, instead of an edit-build-launch loop. It prints `[scan] <N> matches:` + one `  <module>+0x<relOffset>` line per match to the `~` overlay (and the concise `[scan 'kcdx_scan']` lines to `kcdx-dev.log` via `scan_engine::RunScan`); a missing/malformed argv prints the teaching usage / parse-error line, never a silent no-op or crash. It is a **console verb, NOT a `kcdx.*` surface** — single-surface by design (no Lua/C++ parity mirror owed; it IS the cross-surface in-game tool). The Lua `kcdx.scan{...}` workbench (cap-32) is a separate, unchanged runtime-scan surface. |
| Channels | Pure Lua (`plugin.lua`, no DLL) — the test DRIVES the engine console command via `kcdx.console.execute(line)` (CryEngine `IConsole::ExecuteString`, synchronous on the main thread), the same round-trip cap-26 proves against a Lua-registered command. NO C++/Lua parity peer: `kcdx_scan` is a console command, not a `kcdx.*` call with a binding — single-surface, so no NYI mirror is owed. |
| Engine status | LIVE — `kcdx_scan` ships (Register at `console::Init`); this is its regression coverage. |
| Test plugin | [`cap-70-scan-console/`](cap-70-scan-console/) — pure-Lua (no `CMakeLists.txt` entry needed, like cap-26 / cap-32 / cap-68). |
| Auto-pass check | CAP-70-dispatch + CAP-70-badargv are `console`-mode (the console surface must be armed — fired at `input_loaded`); CAP-70-result is `boot-only` (machine-checkable, no overlay). **CAP-70-dispatch**: `kcdx.console.execute('kcdx_scan WHGame.dll "41 03 EC 33 DF 41 03 45 CC C4 E2 50 F2 FA 03 C3"')` returns `true` — the engine console surface accepted + dispatched the kcdx_scan command (the pattern is a deep-interior `.text`-UNIQUE AOB at `WHGame.dll+0x9800`, verified EXACTLY 1 match — DETOUR-IMMUNE because it sits ~4 KB into its containing function, so no co-resident hook's 5-byte entry detour can reach it and no plugin byte-rewrites it, so it still resolves to 1 at `input_loaded`, AFTER the apply pass). Auto-pass on `execute == true`; this holds regardless of how CryEngine tokenizes the quoted pattern (the dispatch is proven either way). FALSIFIABLE: kcdx_scan not registered, or surface not armed → `execute` returns `false` → FAIL. **CAP-70-badargv**: `kcdx.console.execute('kcdx_scan')` (missing module + pattern) returns `true` — the registered command dispatched and ran its argc<3 fail-loud usage-line path without crashing the dispatch (`execute` reports whether the surface dispatched the line, independent of the command's own argv validation). FALSIFIABLE: `execute` returns `false` (not registered) or the game destabilises (the bad-argv path crashed) → FAIL. **CAP-70-result**: asserts the scan FINDING, not just dispatch. `kcdx.scan{ pattern = the same deep-interior AOB, module = "WHGame.dll" }` returns a table with `count == 1`, `matches[1].module == "WHGame.dll"`, and `matches[1].offset` a positive number. The kcdx.scan{} verb and the kcdx_scan console command share `scan_engine::RunScan`, so this exercises the identical resolve path with a machine-checkable return (kcdx_scan paints to the overlay; kcdx.scan{} returns a table) — proving the resolve path FINDS the verified site, a regression catch the `execute==true` dispatch row cannot make (a registered command that finds nothing still returns true). It does NOT assert an exact RVA: `matches[1].offset` is the module-relative offset (~0x9800 on the verified build), which shifts per game build, so an exact compare is flaky-by-construction (same discipline as CAP-32-resolve, which also refuses a per-build absolute address). FALSIFIABLE: `count != 1` (site stopped resolving / scan regressed) or wrong module/non-positive-number offset (resolved the wrong site) → FAIL. |
| Manual observable | The painted `~`-overlay output is the manual eyeball for the two `console` rows (human-perceptual, no machine signal — the acceptance-signal split: assert the dispatch, eyeball the paint). **CAP-70-dispatch**: open `~` and confirm `[scan] 1 matches:` + a `WHGame.dll+0x...` line (the deep-interior `.text`-unique site). **CAP-70-badargv**: open `~` and confirm the usage line `kcdx_scan <module> "<AOB pattern>"  (e.g. ...)` is painted. The "1 matches" observable rests on a deep-interior `.text`-unique site that is detour-immune through the apply pass, so the count is deterministic at `input_loaded` timing regardless of which plugins co-reside. **CAP-70-result**: none — `boot-only`, fully machine-checkable from the `kcdx.scan{}` return; no overlay, no player gesture. |
| Last result | ✅ LIVE (kcdx-dev 2026-06-01 19:52): all three PASS. `[scan 'kcdx_scan'] pattern matches: 1` at `WHGame.dll+0x9800`; CAP-70-dispatch + CAP-70-badargv `verdict=PASS` (console dispatch + fail-loud paths); CAP-70-result `verdict=PASS` (`kcdx.scan{}` resolved the deep-interior AOB to count==1 at `input_loaded`). The detour-immune-site fix (KI-0002) resolved the prior 0-match at input_loaded. |
| Notes | The machine auto-pass for the two `console` rows is "execute returned true" (the command dispatched), NOT a passive registration check — cap-26 is the template for driving a console command from Lua and asserting the round-trip. CAP-70-result adds a `boot-only` result-assertion: it asserts `count==1` (+ module + positive-number offset) for the verified deep-interior AOB via `kcdx.scan{}` over the shared `scan_engine::RunScan` resolve path — proving the scan FINDS the verified site, a real regression catch the `execute==true` dispatch row cannot make (a registered command that finds nothing still returns true). It does NOT assert an exact RVA: the module-relative offset (~0x9800) shifts per game build, so an exact compare is flaky-by-construction — the precision-safe shape (count + module + offset-is-a-positive-number) is the correct reading (mirrors CAP-32-resolve). **argv confirmed intact** — a controlled same-launch comparison + static read established that the quoted pattern arrives whole (`argc==3`, arg 2 == the full pattern, parsed to 16 bytes; the diagnostic that proved it is archived in `src/console_commands_scan.cpp`). **LESSON — a scan-finding assertion at `input_loaded` must use a detour-immune site.** An entry-prologue AOB is co-resident-hostage: any plugin that hooks that function installs a 5-byte JMP detour over the prologue at the apply pass and destroys the match before `input_loaded`. This row picked a co-resident-mutated site TWICE — first the outfit-swap AOB (whose tail `...44 8A F0` cap-39 rewrites → `45 31 F6` at the `ApplyZone` pass), then the luaL_openlibs entry AOB (which the co-resident cap-33-author-targets plugin entry-hooks before `input_loaded`). Both correctly found 0, a fixture-stability defect, not a scan bug. The fix is a **deep-interior, operand-free `.text`-unique AOB** (`WHGame.dll+0x9800`, 4 KB into its function): hook-immune by construction (no 5-byte entry detour reaches the window) and rewritten by nobody, so it stays 1 match through the apply pass regardless of co-residents. cap-69 is the highest prior cap row; cap-70 is the next free id. |

Its value is a small mode (kcdx's launcher sets 0 via user.cfg; default 2 otherwise), so the rows assert the read SUCCEEDED + a plausible small mode (0..3), NOT a hardcoded magic int (the asset-system work showed the value depends on whether kcdx wrote user.cfg). **Float-CVar choice:** `get_float` reads `sys_pakPriority` (not a guessed `e_*` float CVar) — only `sys_pakPriority` is recon-confirmed boot-present; reading a nominally-int CVar as float exercises the GetFVal accessor and is a valid read (assert success, not a value). **Cross-surface parity:** CAP-71-callable and CAP-72-callable read the SAME name via the same engine `cvar::` core, so the two rows MUST report the same observed int value — verified by both rows reporting the same value (the human compares them; separate plugins have no clean shared-runtime value channel). cap-70 is the highest prior cap row; cap-71 is the next free id. |

## CAP-72: `kcdxConsoleInterface::GetCVarInt / GetCVarBool / GetCVarFloat` — read a CVar from C++ (v3 accessors)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | C++ peer of CAP-71. Exercises the CVar-read capability from the C++ surface via the v3 `kcdxConsoleInterface` accessors `GetCVarInt(name, &out)` / `GetCVarBool(name, &out)` / `GetCVarFloat(name, &out)` — the C++ mirror of the Lua `kcdx.cvar.*` surface. Each is a `true`-writes-`*out` / `false`-leaves-`*out`-untouched read: a successful read returns true and writes the value; a miss (CVar absent / surface not ready / null-empty name) returns false and leaves `*out` untouched — so "CVar missing" is distinguishable from a real value of 0. Reads the same engine `cvar::` core the Lua surface uses. The C++ regression coverage for the CVar-read capability. |
| Channels | C++ DLL (`cap-72.dll`). C++/Lua PARITY: CAP-71 is the Lua peer (`kcdx.cvar.*`). |
| Engine status | LIVE — the v3 `GetCVarInt/Bool/Float` accessors (`kcdxConsoleInterface_Version == 3`) ship; this is their regression coverage. |
| Test plugin | [`cap-72-cpp-cvar-read/`](cap-72-cpp-cvar-read/) — C++ DLL (its own `CMakeLists.txt` builds `cap-72.dll`). |
| Auto-pass check | All four rows `boot-only` (machine-checkable from the bool-return + out-param; no overlay). At `InputLoaded`, the **v3 resolve guard** first asserts `K.console != null && GetCVarInt/Bool/Float != null` (the v3 methods resolved) — a null interface/accessor reports every row FAIL "rebuild against v3" (an older engine returns null from `QueryInterface(Console, v3)`). Then: **CAP-72-callable**: `K.console->GetCVarInt("sys_pakPriority", &v)` returns `true` and writes `v` in `0..3` (a plausible small pakPriority mode for a CVar that demonstrably exists). FALSIFIABLE: a `false` return / out-of-range value → FAIL. **CAP-72-float**: `GetCVarFloat("sys_pakPriority", &f)` returns `true` (GetFVal accessor read a CVar that exists; value-agnostic). FALSIFIABLE: `false` → FAIL. **CAP-72-bool**: `GetCVarBool("sys_pakPriority", &b)` returns `true` (= int != 0; value-agnostic). FALSIFIABLE: `false` → FAIL. **CAP-72-miss**: `GetCVarInt("kcdx_nonexistent_cvar_xyz", &v)` with `v` pre-set to a `0x6BADF00D` sentinel returns `false` AND leaves `v` at the sentinel — the no-garbage-write contract. FALSIFIABLE: a `true` return (fabricated value) OR a mutated out-param despite the false return (a garbage write) → FAIL. All report on the first `InputLoaded` fire (one-shot CAS-guarded). |
| Manual observable | None — all four rows are `boot-only` and fully machine-checkable from the report (the bool-return + out-param). A CVar read has a machine-readable result, so there is no perceptual eyeball. |
| Last result | PENDING (initial launch). |
| Notes | Mirrors cap-69's C++ readiness-gated shape (the one-shot CAS `InputLoaded` listener), but the rows are `boot-only` not `console`: a CVar read self-reports its result fully (unlike a console print, whose paint is perceptual). Requests v3 of `kcdxConsoleInterface` (the version carrying the three CVar accessors, appended below the `--- APPEND-ONLY BELOW ---` marker — the v2→v3 bump for the CVar-read surface; `plugin interface ABI is append-only`). The v3 resolve guard is the cap-69-style version-mismatch FAIL: a plugin built against a newer header but loaded by a pre-v3 engine gets a safe FAIL "rebuild against v3", never a null-accessor deref. **Cross-surface parity:** CAP-72-callable and CAP-71-callable read the SAME `sys_pakPriority` via the same engine `cvar::` core, so the two rows MUST report the same observed int value (the human compares both rows — separate plugins have no clean shared-runtime value channel). **CAP-72-miss** is the strongest falsifiable row: the `0x6BADF00D` out-param sentinel proves the engine's false return leaves the out untouched — a fabricated value OR a garbage write both go red. cap-71 is the highest prior cap row; cap-72 is the next free id. |

## CAP-73: asset replacement is DECLARED, not inferred — the no-code sidecar (`replaces.toml`)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The no-code declarative replacement surface. A file in a plugin's `assets/` dir overlays a vanilla (or another mod's) asset ONLY when a co-located `replaces.toml` sidecar DECLARES it — existence ≠ replacement (asset design §4.1). The prior implicit-by-path keying (a file auto-applying if its path coincided with a vanilla path) is REPLACED by declaration-required keying: the overlay map is keyed by the **declared target** the author names in `replaces`, not by the file's own path-in-`assets`. An undeclared file replaces nothing. Three falsifiable rows: a declared replacement is keyed; a missing/malformed target fails LOUD (AP14); two declarations of one target resolve by load order with the §4.4 conflict line. |
| Channels | Pure-TOML sidecars (no Lua, no DLL — the no-code path). The programmatic `kcdx.assets.replace/declare` code path is a LATER phase. |
| Engine status | LIVE — `replaces.toml` parse (`src/asset_sidecar.cpp`) + declaration-required `BuildOverlayMap` keying (`src/asset_overlay.cpp`). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-73-asset-sidecar-applies/`](cap-73-asset-sidecar-applies/) (declared-applies), [`cap-73-asset-sidecar-missing/`](cap-73-asset-sidecar-missing/) (missing-target loud-fail), [`cap-73-asset-sidecar-conflict-a/`](cap-73-asset-sidecar-conflict-a/) + [`cap-73-asset-sidecar-conflict-b/`](cap-73-asset-sidecar-conflict-b/) (same-target conflict pair) — all pure-Lua-free TOML-only plugins (no `CMakeLists.txt`). |
| Auto-pass check | All `boot-only`, confirmed from `kcdx-dev.log` at boot (the sidecar parse + map-build run on the worker thread before the menu; no player gesture, no overlay eyeball — the parse-path coverage). **CAP-73-declared-applies**: `cap-73-applies`' `replaces.toml` declares `Libs/UI/Textures/CAP73Placeholder.dds`; the agent greps `ASSET_OVERLAY` `overlay_entry vpath=libs/ui/textures/cap73placeholder.dds winner=cap_73_applies` (the declaration was parsed AND keyed by its declared target). FALSIFIABLE: that line absent → FAIL (the declaration was not parsed / not keyed). **CAP-73-missing-target**: `cap-73-missing`' `replaces.toml` row names a `file` that does not exist; the agent greps `ASSET_SIDECAR` `rejected ... plugin=cap_73_missing ... reason=...file...does not exist...`. FALSIFIABLE: no `rejected` line for the plugin → FAIL (the bad target was SILENTLY dropped — the exact silent-orphan AP15 bar). **CAP-73-conflict**: `cap-73-conflict-a` (priority 30, earlier) and `-b` (priority 70, later) both declare `Data/cap73_shared_target.xml`; the agent greps `ASSET_OVERLAY` `Asset replacement conflict on target 'Data/cap73_shared_target.xml': plugin 'cap_73_conflict_a' wins ...; plugin 'cap_73_conflict_b' is suppressed ...`. FALSIFIABLE: no conflict line → FAIL (both silently kept / one silently dropped). |
| Manual observable | None — all three rows are `boot-only` and machine-checkable from the boot log. (The in-game perceptual render of a declared `.dds` overlay is the throwaway `probe-asset-overlay` fixture's job + step 10's permanent home; cap-73 proves the SIDECAR PARSE + map-keying, which is fully observable in the log without a launch gesture.) |
| Last result | PENDING (initial launch). |
| Notes | **Sidecar filename/shape:** the sidecar is `replaces.toml` (NOT `targets.toml`) — the asset-replacement concern is distinct from the code-target sidecar (a directory may carry both); it mirrors the `targets.toml` idiom (co-located, `[[asset]]` rows ≈ `[[target]]` rows). **Scope = placement (§4.2):** a `replaces.toml` sharing a directory with exactly one asset needs no `file` key (file-scope); a directory-level one names its `file` (the more it abstracts, the more each row specifies). **Vanilla-existence oracle scoped out:** the missing-target check this step enforces is on the row's named `file` + the form-ambiguity cases (both-forms / incomplete-pair / unknown-key / wrong-type) — all checkable at boot without the engine. Proactively calling the engine's existence leaves (`IsFileInPak` id 153 / `DoesFileExistOnDisk` id 154) to validate a `replaces` VANILLA-VPATH at build-time is an unverified runtime unknown (the §8 ctor-vs-first-read install-timing is itself a build probe) — passed `nullptr` (disabled) this step, the resolver's MISS path is the backstop, and wiring the live oracle is a scoped follow-up gated on that probe. **`name`-publish scoped out:** a sidecar `name` (US-5 publish) is parsed + reported but NOT published — the asset-namespace store is a later phase (no store exists to publish into). **Cross-mod targets scoped out:** a published-name `replaces` or a `replaces_plugin`+`replaces_path` pair is a US-3/US-4 reference (not a vanilla vpath the engine requests); parsed + reported (`overlay_decl_scoped_out`), consumed by a later phase, not keyed into this step's resolver lookup. cap-72 is the highest prior cap row; cap-73 is the next free id. |

## CAP-74: the navigable cross-plugin namespace — `kcdx.plugin.<author>.<plugin>.*` (`__index` chain)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The general cross-plugin NAVIGATION primitive (asset design §6, the US-3 enabler). `kcdx.plugin` gains an `__index` resolver chain: `kcdx.plugin.<author>` resolves the author segment to an author resolver, whose `__index` resolves `<plugin>` to a plugin handle — each dot a resolution hop against engine-side namespace data (`g_plugins`). The SAME chained-`__index` mechanism `kcdx.hook.<name>` uses, NOT a new architecture. It reads as native dotted Lua (`kcdx.plugin.redmoon.outfit_swap.assets.get_by_name("shirt")`), no quoted-namespace ceremony, all under the one `kcdx` global. ADDITIVE — the `kcdx.plugin.is_rejected("author.plugin")` function-call surface is unchanged (a function member shadows the `__index`). This step ships the navigation PRIMITIVE (author + plugin segments resolve to a handle); the `.assets.*` LEAF it fronts is a LATER step's deliverable (a resolved handle's `.assets` is a navigation miss until that surface lands). |
| Channels | Pure Lua (`plugin.lua`, no DLL). C++ NYI mirror is the C++ cross-plugin reference surface (its shape — whether C++ even has a navigable analog, or reaches cross-plugin by an owner-arg call — is the C++-mirror step's design question, NOT settled here; see the asset-system phase-2 step-9 doc). |
| Engine status | LIVE — the navigable-namespace resolver chain (`src/lua_bind_plugin.cpp` — `Lua_PluginTableIndex` / `Lua_PluginAuthorIndex` / `Lua_PluginHandleIndex`, registered as the `kcdx.plugin` table's `__index` metamethod in `bind()`). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-74-plugin-namespace-nav/`](cap-74-plugin-namespace-nav/) — pure-Lua (no `CMakeLists.txt` entry needed, like cap-68 / cap-70 / cap-71). |
| Auto-pass check | All four rows `boot-only` — every check is a synchronous read of the in-process plugin registry at plugin load (no player gesture, no perceptual overlay; the navigation result IS the machine signal). **CAP-74-resolves**: navigating `kcdx.plugin.ts.cap_74_plugin_namespace_nav` (THIS plugin's own identity — self is guaranteed loaded, the reliable known-present target) does not raise and yields a non-nil handle. **CAP-74-bad-author**: `kcdx.plugin.<nonexistent>.x` RAISES at the second hop (the bad author resolved to nil → the teaching index-error). **CAP-74-bad-plugin**: `kcdx.plugin.ts.<nonexistent>.x` RAISES (real author, bad plugin → nil → teaching error) — the second hop's miss path proven independently. **CAP-74-is-rejected-coexists**: `is_rejected` stays a callable function returning the query shape (the navigation `__index` is additive). |
| Manual observable | None — all four rows are `boot-only` and fully machine-checkable from the report. A navigation hop returns a handle / raises / returns nil — a machine-readable result, no perceptual eyeball. |
| Last result | PENDING (initial launch). |
| Notes | **Navigation target = self-identity (`ts.cap_74_plugin_namespace_nav`):** self > engine > other means a plugin's OWN `(author, plugin)` is guaranteed loaded and resolvable — the only navigation target reliable across any session's plugin set (an "other" plugin may or may not be loaded). **Miss idiom matches `kcdx.hook`, NOT a third idiom:** a non-existent `<author>`/`<plugin>` segment resolves to `nil`, so the next dotted access raises Lua's stock "attempt to index a nil value (field '…')" naming the typoed segment — the same navigation-miss idiom the `kcdx.hook.<name>` smart-resolver established (cap-59's `CAP-59-invalid-mode-nil` asserts the same shape). is_rejected's `(nil, err)` is the bad-ARG idiom — a different situation. **`(author, plugin)` resolution keys on BOTH components:** `HandleOf()` keys on `[plugin].name` alone and cannot disambiguate two authors reusing a plugin name; the resolver matches author AND plugin against `g_plugins`. **The `.assets` leaf is a later step:** a resolved plugin handle's `__index` returns nil for `.assets` until the asset surface attaches the leaf — so this step's coverage is the author+plugin segment resolution + the miss teaching error, not the leaf return. cap-73 is the highest prior cap row; cap-74 is the next free id. |

## CAP-75: `kcdx.assets.*` — the author's code-side asset surface (get_by_path / get_by_name / declare / register / replace) (Lua)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The five `kcdx.assets.*` verbs (asset design §5). `get_by_path(path)` resolves the CALLING plugin's own asset (no owner prefix) to a loadable on-disk path — a PURE READ (US-2). `declare(name, file)` publishes `<author>.<plugin>.<name>` → the file's resolved path (US-5); `get_by_name(name)` resolves a published name back to a loadable path. `register(vpath, file)` adds a runtime vpath → file overlay (US-6); `replace(target, with)` registers a runtime replacement keyed by a VANILLA-PATH target — both write the §5.1 runtime-overlay store and take effect for opens THEREAFTER. A PACKED cross-mod `replace` target (`<author>.<plugin>.<bare>`) needs cross-mod resolution (name → the other mod's serve-vpath, §5.3) which lands in a LATER step; until then it returns a TEACHING ERROR (the resolver only looks up vanilla vpaths, so a packed-name overlay write could never be hit — a silent non-serve, AP13/AP14). All four runtime verbs resolve `file` through the caller's `assets/` (the same `ResolveAssetPath` `get_by_path` uses); a bad path / name / target → a TEACHING ERROR naming it, never a silent nil (AP14). The cross-plugin forms `kcdx.plugin.<author>.<plugin>.assets.get_by_path / .get_by_name` resolve the NAVIGATED plugin's read through the cap-74 namespace (the `.assets` leaf binds the read verb to the navigated `(author, plugin)`); `register`/`declare`/`replace` are own-namespace-only (an author writes into their OWN namespace), correctly absent from the cross-plugin leaf. |
| Channels | Pure Lua (`plugin.lua`, no DLL). C++/Lua PARITY: the C++ mirror (`kcdxAssetInterface` — `GetByPath` / `GetByName` / `Declare` / `Register` / `Replace`) is now BUILT — its parity rows are **CAP-76** (the C++ peer of these rows); `docs/cpp/assets.md` is flipped from NYI to Built. |
| Engine status | LIVE — `kcdx.assets.get_by_path` / `.get_by_name` / `.declare` / `.register` / `.replace` (`src/lua_bind_assets.cpp` — registered as the `kcdx.assets` domain in `bind()`). The runtime stores are `src/asset_namespace.{h,cpp}` (RCU atomic-pointer snapshots: the runtime-overlay store for register/replace + the published-name store for declare/get_by_name). The two resolver hooks (`src/asset_overlay.cpp` HOOK 1 / HOOK 2) consult the runtime-overlay snapshot lock-free ALONGSIDE the build-time `g_overlayMap` on a build-time MISS. The cross-plugin `.assets` leaf (`PushPluginAssetsDomain`, dispatched from `src/lua_bind_plugin.cpp` `Lua_PluginHandleIndex`) exposes get_by_path + get_by_name. |
| Test plugin | [`cap-75-assets-get-by-path/`](cap-75-assets-get-by-path/) — pure-Lua (no `CMakeLists.txt` entry needed, like cap-71 / cap-74). Ships a real asset `assets/icons/marker.txt` so the own + cross reads resolve a real file. |
| Auto-pass check | All nine rows `boot-only` — every check is a synchronous in-process store write+read + the on-disk `assets/` tree at plugin load (no player gesture; the resolved path / teaching error IS the machine signal). **CAP-75-own / CAP-75-missing / CAP-75-cross** — get_by_path own / missing-teaches / cross-plugin (as before). **CAP-75-declare** — `declare("shirt", "icons/marker.txt")` then `get_by_name("shirt")` resolves back to the same file's path (a published-name store round-trip). FALSIFIABLE: declare nil for a real file / get_by_name nil after declaring / wrong file → FAIL. **CAP-75-getbyname-missing** — `get_by_name("never_declared_name")` returns `(nil, err)` naming it. FALSIFIABLE: non-nil / bare nil / err not naming the name → FAIL. **CAP-75-getbyname-cross** — `kcdx.plugin.ts.cap_75_assets_get_by_path.assets.get_by_name("shirt")` resolves the §6 cross-plugin published name through the namespace. FALSIFIABLE: chain raises / nil / wrong file → FAIL. **CAP-75-register** — `register(vpath, real file)` returns the loadable path (overlay written); `register(vpath, missing file)` teaches loud. FALSIFIABLE: nil for a real file / non-nil-or-bare-nil for a missing file → FAIL. **CAP-75-replace** — `replace(vanilla-path, real file)` returns the path (overlay keyed by target); `replace(target, missing file)` teaches loud. FALSIFIABLE: same shape. **CAP-75-replace-crossmod-teach** — `replace("redmoon.outfit.belt", real file)` (a PACKED cross-mod target) returns `(nil, err)` naming the packed target, never a store write + path (cross-mod resolution is a later step, §5.3 — the entry could never be hit, AP13/AP14). FALSIFIABLE: a non-nil return / bare nil / err not naming the target → FAIL. |
| Manual observable | The boot-only Lua rows above are machine-checkable from the report. Two rows need an agent log-read: the register/replace SERVE (engine opens a registered vpath → kcdx's file) is the `in-game` **CAP-75-register-serve** row, confirmed by reading `ASSET_OVERLAY overlay_opened ... source=runtime` after an in-game open. **CAP-75-replace-boot-asset-warn** (KI-0005) is `boot-only` but agent-log-read: a `register`/`replace` of a vpath the engine opened at boot (`Libs/UI/Textures/KCDLogo.dds`) emits a one-time teaching warn — the agent greps `ASSET_RUNTIME runtime_overlay_boot_asset vpath="libs/ui/textures/kcdlogo.dds"` after the launch-to-menu run (its ABSENCE for a boot-opened target is the silent no-op KI-0005 closes → FAIL). Both are machine signals in the log, not perceptual eyeballs. |
| Last result | PENDING (initial launch). |
| Notes | **Loadable path = the absolute disk path the seam serves:** the four runtime verbs resolve `file` through the SAME `folderPath / assetsEntrypointRel` join + `..`-reject + `is_regular_file` check `get_by_path` uses (`ResolveAssetPath`, `src/lua_bind_assets.cpp`), so a declared name / registered overlay always points at a real loadable file. **Own-namespace forms take no owner prefix:** `ResolveCaller` (`OwningPluginForCurrentCall`, `src/lua_registry.h`) resolves the calling plugin off the Lua callstack — the engine knows who you are; an anonymous caller (console / ad-hoc Lua) gets a teaching `(nil, err)`. **Concurrency (§5.1):** the runtime-overlay store is an immutable RCU snapshot behind `std::atomic<const Snapshot*>` — the writer (register/replace, one-off author calls on the Lua main thread) builds a new snapshot and store-RELEASEs the pointer; the hot resolver hooks load-ACQUIRE and read a never-mutated snapshot (wait-free, allocation-free). The build-time `g_overlayMap` stays not-mutated-after-build + lock-free — UNTOUCHED. Old snapshots are retained-for-session (a bounded tiny leak — register/replace are rare). **Take-effect = thereafter** (§3 US-6): a register/replace affects opens AFTER the call; no re-resolve of open handles. cap-74 is the highest prior cap row; cap-75 is the next free id. |

Append-only ABI: the new enum value at the END, the new struct, the new QueryInterface case — no existing field/enum/offset shifted. |

Each self-reports at `kcdxPlugin_Load` (the runtime asset stores are reachable at this init phase — the same path the Lua binder reaches synchronously). Each is a PARITY assertion on the `const char*` return. **CAP-76-own**: `GetByPath(self, "icons/marker.txt")` returns a non-null loadable path ending with the relative path. FALSIFIABLE: a non-null return → FAIL. No player input. |

## CAP-77: a served handle-consumed (`.lua`) overlay EXECUTES on a save load

| Field | Value |

## COMP-18: foreign-hook prologue classifier (foreign-hook DETECTION, hook-backend-marriage §6.1)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The prologue classifier (`src/foreign_hook_detect.{h,cpp}`) — before kcdx installs a function-entry hook, it reads the target's prologue and classifies it: **Clean** (real game instructions → install normally), **KcdxTrampoline** (an E9/FF25 jump into a range kcdx ITSELF allocated → already in a kcdx chain), **Foreign** (an E9/FF25 jump pointing OUTSIDE every kcdx-owned trampoline range → another mod hooked it first), or **Unknown** (an E9/FF-family opcode the decoder could not resolve to a valid 5-byte E9 / 14-byte FF25 form → conservative, never chained). This is the DETECTION half of foreign-hook coexistence; CHAINING onto a Foreign verdict is comp-NN (step 8). The kcdx-vs-foreign discriminator is kcdx's OWN trampoline ranges (`src/kcdx_trampoline_registry.{h,cpp}`), fed at install by the three producers (the `SafetyhookBackend` InlineHook trampoline, the branch-pool reservations, the safetyhook MidHook adapter) — NOT a query into safetyhook (its Allocator does not expose its ranges; `m_memory` is private). |
| Channels | Engine self-test (`src/foreign_hook_detect_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-66 / cap-80 / cap-81. The classifier is engine-internal (it reads a target VA's prologue bytes — a boot/install-plumbing concern, no `kcdx.*` author surface), so it self-reports from engine code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult`. Single-surface (engine-internal). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/foreign_hook_detect.{h,cpp}` + `src/kcdx_trampoline_registry.{h,cpp}` ship the classifier + the kcdx-owned-range source; `src/hook_chain.cpp` wires `DetectForeignBeforeInstall` into the Add/AddC first-touch install (as of step 8 the Foreign branch logs the CHAIN and the normal install relocates the foreign jump — see COMP-19; the existing clean-prologue install is unchanged). Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). |
| Test plugin | none — engine self-test (`src/foreign_hook_detect_selftest.cpp`, `RunSelfTestOnce()` one-shot from the per-tick self-report block right after cap-81). Feeds SYNTHETIC prologues (static byte buffers at known VAs) through `Classify` + the E9/FF25 byte decode — no live target needed (the classifier reads bytes kcdx owns the address of). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. One row, **comp-18-foreign-classifier**, five synthetic prologues: clean game bytes (`48 89 5C 24 …`)→Clean; an E9 jump into a REGISTERED kcdx range→KcdxTrampoline (+ the E9 target-VA decode is exact); a foreign E9 into an unregistered range→Foreign; a foreign FF25 into an unregistered range→Foreign (+ the FF25 absolute-target decode is exact); an unrecognized FF shape (`FF D0` = call rax)→Unknown. No player input — runs on the first suite tick. |
| Last result | pending — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL comp-18-foreign-classifier` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | The classifier distinguishes a clean prologue, a jump into a kcdx-owned trampoline range, and a FOREIGN E9/FF25 jump — and treats an unrecognized shape conservatively. **FAILS if** a clean prologue is classified a jump, **or** an E9 into a registered kcdx range is classified Foreign (the discriminator failed to recognize kcdx's own range), **or** a foreign E9 / foreign FF25 is classified Clean or KcdxTrampoline (the core detection claim — a foreign hook NOT detected), **or** the E9/FF25 byte decode computes the wrong target VA, **or** an unrecognized FF shape is classified Foreign/KcdxTrampoline instead of Unknown (an unknown prologue mis-chained — AP14: an unrecognized shape must fail conservative + loud, never silently chained). |
| Notes | **Detection-only this step** (chaining onto a Foreign verdict is step 8 / comp-19). The first-touch install (`hook_chain` Add/AddC, FindChain==null) runs the classifier BEFORE `InstallRuntime`; a Foreign verdict is logged + (as of step 8) CHAINED onto by the normal install. The classifier never runs for an existing-chain append and never alters the clean-prologue common path. **FF25 encoding:** `FF 25 00 00 00 00` (FF /4 JMP r/m64 + the `25` modrm selecting [rip+disp32], disp=0) then the 8-byte absolute target at +6 — exactly safetyhook's far-target fallback. **kcdx-owned-range source:** the SafetyhookBackend InlineHook trampoline (via `hook.trampoline().address()/.size()`) + branch-pool reservations are registered; the **MidHook trampoline range is NOT registered** — safetyhook's `MidHook` does not expose its stub/trampoline publicly (`m_hook`/`m_stub` private) — surfaced as a step-8 finding (narrow gap: a mid jump lands at a mid-function VA, not a function entry a later hook targets). cap-83/cap-81 are the highest prior cap rows; comp-17 is the highest prior comp row; comp-18 is the next free comp id. |

## COMP-19: foreign-hook chaining (foreign-hook COEXISTENCE, hook-backend-marriage §6.2/§6.3)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The CHAINING half of foreign-hook coexistence (US-4, E14/E20; comp-18 proved DETECTION). When kcdx installs a function-entry hook on a target ANOTHER mod already hooked (a foreign E9 in the prologue), BOTH detours fire, in the order **game → kcdx → foreign → original** (kcdx-first load order, §6.3). The chaining is NOT a hand-rolled jump-follower — it falls out of safetyhook's NORMAL install: `InlineHook::create` RELOCATES the foreign prologue jump into kcdx's trampoline with an IP-fixed displacement (`vendor/safetyhook/src/inline_hook.cpp` `e9_hook` — a relative prologue instruction is decoded + relocated with a recomputed `new_disp`), so kcdx's call-original (the JIT slot) runs the foreign mod's detour, which runs the real function (§6.2). A Foreign-classified target takes the SAME `SafetyhookBackend` install a Clean one does — only the log line (and the unrelocatable-shape loud-fail path) differs. **Out of scope (v1, §6.3/§10, NOT built):** a foreign mod that unhooks mid-session or installs AFTER kcdx. Chain-always — no configurable policy (§6.4). |
| Channels | Engine self-test (`src/foreign_hook_chain_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like comp-18 / cap-80 / cap-81. Chaining is engine-internal (it falls out of the `SafetyhookBackend` install path — a boot/install-plumbing concern, no `kcdx.*` author surface), so it self-reports from engine code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult`. Single-surface (engine-internal): the author runs kcdx alongside another mod and gets coexistence for free, with no new declaration. |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/hook_chain.cpp` `DetectForeignBeforeInstall` Foreign branch now logs the CHAIN (the install that follows relocates the foreign jump); `src/safetyhook_backend.cpp` names the unrelocatable-foreign-shape loud-fail in its `UNSUPPORTED_INSTRUCTION_IN_TRAMPOLINE` / `FAILED_TO_DECODE_INSTRUCTION` reason strings (the install fails, the foreign hook survives — AP14, §6.3). The relocation itself is unchanged vendored safetyhook. Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). |
| Test plugin | none — engine self-test (`src/foreign_hook_chain_selftest.cpp`, `RunSelfTestOnce()` one-shot from the per-tick self-report block right after comp-18). The fixture owns BOTH sides (AP1 — no game VA): a kcdx-controlled stub (`kcdx_comp19_chain_target`) is the "real original"; a synthetic FOREIGN detour is installed on it FIRST (a second safetyhook `InlineHook` standing in for the other mod — safetyhook writes a standard 5-byte E9 rel32, the exact foreign prologue jump the relocation chains onto); then kcdx's own detour installs OVER it. Each detour appends a marker to a shared order-log; the call is made in-process. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. One row, **comp-19-foreign-chaining**: install a synthetic foreign E9 on the stub, install kcdx's detour over it, call the stub once. PASS iff the order-log reads exactly `KFO` (kcdx fired first → delegated to the foreign detour via its relocated call-original → the real stub ran) AND the return carries the stub's sentinel (`arg ^ 0x6c13`). Dev-gated (installs real safetyhook detours). No player input — runs on the first suite tick. |
| Last result | pending — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL comp-19-foreign-chaining` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | When kcdx hooks a target a foreign mod already hooked, BOTH detours fire in order game → kcdx → foreign → original. **FAILS if** the order-log is not exactly `KFO` — a missing `K` (kcdx's detour never fired), a missing `F` (kcdx's call-original did NOT run the foreign hook → chaining broke, safetyhook did not relocate the foreign jump), a missing `O` (the real stub never ran), or `FK`/any wrong order (load order inverted) — **or** the return does not carry the stub's sentinel (the chain did not reach the real function). Not a tautology (AP15): every marker must be present AND in order, and the original must be reached through the relocated foreign jump. |
| Notes | **Chaining via safetyhook relocation, not a jump-follower** — kcdx installs normally for a Foreign target; safetyhook's `e9_hook` relocates the foreign E9 into kcdx's trampoline IP-fixed, and that relocated jump IS kcdx's call-original (the foreign detour). **The unrelocatable-foreign-shape path** (a foreign jump safetyhook can't relocate → `UNSUPPORTED_INSTRUCTION_IN_TRAMPOLINE` / `FAILED_TO_DECODE_INSTRUCTION`) fails LOUD via `SafetyhookBackend::enable()`'s error log + the caller's "InstallRuntime failed" reason; the foreign hook is left intact (prologue restored by `reset()`) — never a silent mis-install or corrupted prologue (AP14, §6.3). A standard E9 IS relocatable, so this fixture exercises the chaining SUCCESS path; the loud-fail path is covered by review + the named error strings (a synthetic unrelocatable foreign jump is not cleanly constructible without inventing an unrelocatable prologue, which would itself be brittle). **Out-of-scope NOT built:** foreign-unhooks-later, foreign-installs-after-kcdx (§6.3/§10 documented limitations). cap-83/cap-81 are the highest prior cap rows; comp-18 is the highest prior comp row; comp-19 is the next free comp id. |

## CAP-84: survival per-kind DISPATCH + Ambiguous + the 5 STATIC non-function checks + anchor-dependency ordering + the 3.3 startup verification pass

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The survival checker (`src/survival.{h,cpp}`) is a PER-KIND DISPATCH: one entry point `SurvivalCheck(Payload{kind,...}, rva, derivesFrom, dll)` dispatched on `Kind`, with a kind-discriminated `Payload` (function kinds carry `contentHash`+`length`; callsite/instruction_anchor carry `aob`+`aobMask`; string_anchor `anchorString`+`expectUnique`; data_slot `rule`; vtable_base `slotCount` — the D22 folded survival columns). **Step 3.1** built the dispatch + payload + the function-hash path (verdict UNCHANGED) + the `Ambiguous` status (D31a — a callsite AOB matching >1 `.text` site). **Step 3.2 (this step)** implements the 5 STATIC non-function checks under the dispatch, ALL on-disk (D25): **callsite** re-matches the stored AOB in on-disk `.text` (unique→Unchanged, zero→Changed, multi→**Ambiguous**); **string_anchor** confirms the literal in on-disk `.rdata` (+ a unique `.text` LEA xref when `expectUnique`); **instruction_anchor** re-runs the resolver chain (`.rdata` string → `.text` LEA→string → instruction-shape match); **data_slot** re-runs the derivation (`disp32@<id>` forward-follow or `<id>±0xNN` offset) and confirms it lands in on-disk `.data` (NO content hash — `.data` holds relocated pointers); **vtable_base** reads N qwords (`slotCount`) and confirms each is a plausible relocated `.text` pointer. PLUS the **anchor-dependency ordering** (`CheckOrdered` — a row SET checked anchors-first; a dependent whose anchor came back Changed transitively short-circuits to `CannotCheck`/`anchor_changed`, never silently re-derived). `vtable_index` stays a DEFINED deferral (`CannotCheck`/`vtable_index_deferred` — slot-target body-hash population waits on the runtime-vtable path). **Step 3.3 (this step)** adds the **startup verification pass** (`src/survival_verify.{h,cpp}`, `RunStartupVerification`): ONCE at startup over the curated USER set (every `refdb::ForEachCached` entity), it combines the **on-disk version-applicability** check (REUSE the 3.1/3.2 dispatch — match→applicable) with the **live-image reachability** range test (does the engine-resolved VA land in live `.text`? — `pe::IsVaInLiveText`, a RANGE TEST, **NOT a live-body hash** per Probe 0.4: the live image is relocated + kcdx-detoured) into one of four D25 verdicts per row — `resolves_works` / `wrong_target` / `dead` / `cannot_check` — plus the **matched `address_version_id`** (D34 attribution). NO JSON report (Phase 4) and NO batch DB mutation (Phase 5) here — the pass returns the structured per-row result in-process. The cross-impl agreement test is 3.4. |

Engine reports via `src/survival_dispatch_selftest.cpp`, `RunSelfTestOnce()` one-shot from the per-tick self-report block right after cap-60. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. One row, **cap-84-survival-dispatch**, nine sub-checks: (1) FUNCTION VERDICT UNCHANGED — dispatch's function path == legacy `Result` over 3 deterministic cases + 1 REAL on-disk SaveGame check (DEGRADED if the DB lacks `content_hash`); (2) vtable_index DEFERRAL — `CannotCheck`/`vtable_index_deferred`; (3) AMBIGUOUS REPORTABLE — `vcc::FuncStatus::Ambiguous` round-trips the cache codec + the pass maps a non-byte ref to CannotCheck; (4) CALLSITE — a synthetic improbable AOB → Changed, an ultra-common 2-byte AOB → **Ambiguous**, a REAL curated `IsInCombat_callsite_26b` → Unchanged (DEGRADED if absent); (5) STRING_ANCHOR — a synthetic absent literal → Changed, a REAL curated `string_exec_autoexec_cfg` → Unchanged (DEGRADED if absent); (6) TRANSITIVE ANCHOR-CHANGED — a 2-row set through `CheckOrdered` (a Changed string_anchor + a dependent) MUST short-circuit the dependent to `CannotCheck`/`anchor_changed`; **(7) REACHABILITY RANGE TEST** — `pe::IsVaInLiveText` reads an off-image VA (base−1MB) + VA 0 as NOT in live `.text`, and a real engine-resolved curated function VA AS in `.text` (DEGRADED if WHGame unmapped); **(8) STARTUP VERIFICATION PASS** — `RunStartupVerification` yields a defined verdict per swept row (a loaded refdb → non-empty sweep), and a known-good curated function (SaveGame) reading `resolves_works` surfaces its matched `address_version_id` (D34) while a `wrong_target` carries none (DEGRADED if WHGame unmapped / DB lacks the row); **(9) VERDICT-COMBINATION DISCRIMINATION** — a function payload with a synthetic non-matching `content_hash` (0x33×32) at SaveGame's real rva reads on-disk Changed (→ `wrong_target`), never a false Unchanged (→ `resolves_works`). The on-disk + reachability sub-checks DEGRADE-pass when WHGame.dll is not mapped (a non-game host); the synthetic Changed/Ambiguous cases need no DB; the real `resolves_works`/`dead`/`wrong_target` discrimination is confirmed at the live launch. No player input — runs on the first suite tick. |
| Last result | (pending launch) — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-84-survival-dispatch` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | Each kind verdicts correctly AND a Changed anchor transitively blocks its dependents AND the 3.3 startup pass combines version-applicability + reachability into the right D25 verdict. **FAILS if** the function path diverges from legacy, **or** `vtable_index` returns a non-deferred verdict, **or** `Ambiguous` does not survive the pass+codec, **or** a callsite AOB that cannot occur reads Unchanged / a multi-hit AOB does NOT read Ambiguous, **or** an absent string literal reads Unchanged, **or** a dependent whose anchor is Changed is NOT blocked with `anchor_changed`, **or** `pe::IsVaInLiveText` reads an off-image/null VA as in-`.text` (or a genuinely-good function VA as dead), **or** `RunStartupVerification` drops the whole curated set / a `resolves_works` carries no matched id / a `wrong_target` carries one, **or** a synthetic non-matching fingerprint reads Unchanged (a false `resolves_works`) (AP14 — a fail-loud verdict, never a false Unchanged/resolves_works). Not a tautology (AP15): each sub-check reads the ACTUAL on-disk binary / live module / `CheckOrdered` / `RunStartupVerification` output and asserts a SPECIFIC verdict, not "not Unchanged". The reachability check is a range test against live `.text`, **NOT a live-body hash** (Probe 0.4 — re-introducing one is a known wrong design). |
| Notes | **On-disk-vs-live (D25 SETTLED, NOT re-opened):** all 5 static checks read WHGame.dll's ON-DISK backing file (the same read the function-hash kind uses); the existing `pe_helpers` `ModuleView` primitives are live-image, so 3.2 added ON-DISK analogues (`OnDiskSections` + the filtered accessors) parsing the file's own headers the way `RvaToFileOffsetOnDisk` already does — a thin adaptation of the existing on-disk RVA→offset path, NOT a from-scratch section walker. **The anchor-DAG lives in `CheckOrdered`, NOT the single-row entry (a real design call, surfaced):** a derivation needs its anchor's RESOLVED address, which only exists after the anchor is checked; the single `SurvivalCheck(payload,...)` has neither, so the DAG ordering is a NEW set-level entry point that threads each anchor's resolved RVA + verdict onto the dependent's `Payload` (appended `hasAnchor`/`anchorChanged`/`anchorResolvedRva`). It is NOT bolted into production `survival_pass::RunPass` (which carries only function-row `TouchedRef`s, no kind/payload/derivesFrom — that wiring is 3.3). **FLAGGED — threaded resolved RVA == the anchor's STORED rva when Unchanged:** correct within one game version (the survival scope); full relocation-tracking (threading a relocated anchor's re-found RVA) would need the single-row checks to RETURN their resolved RVA — a richer future capability. **FLAGGED edge case (spec-silent):** a present string_anchor with `expectUnique` whose `.text` xref count ≠ 1 → Ambiguous (the bytes ARE present, but the unique property the resolver depends on broke — chosen over Changed/Unchanged per the design's Ambiguous semantics + the fail-toward-CannotCheck/Changed posture). **pe_helpers gaps built (all thin, additive — no name collision):** G1 `FindDisp32Forward` lifts `FindLeaXrefsTo`'s 4-byte-LE decode forward; G2 `WritableDataSections` mirrors `ReadOnlyDataSections`; G4 `IsTextPointerOnDisk` subtracts the on-disk `ImageBase` then range-tests against executable sections. **Reuse (not new infra):** the callsite/instruction-shape AOB match reuses `patch::Pattern`+`FindAllInBuffer` (G3 — the wildcard mask is already there); the string search + LEA-xref mirror `patch_engine.cpp`'s `ResolveAnchor` chain on-disk. **Impact:** `survival_pass.cpp` UNTOUCHED (the legacy 4-arg entry survives; the 3.3 startup pass is a DISTINCT sweep in `survival_verify.cpp`, never a change to the plugin-targeted pass); `patch_engine`/`scan_engine` reused read-only; the hook-backend files NOT touched. **3.3 (this step):** `survival_verify.{h,cpp}` (NEW TU + CMakeLists row) + `pe::IsVaInLiveText` (additive) + `refdb::CachedAddressVersionId` (one additive read accessor for the D34 matched id — no public resolve struct widened). **3.3 FLAGGED (spec-silent, fail-toward-cannot_check/wrong_target):** (a) reachability "lands in `.text`" = a VA range test over the LIVE `ExecutableSections` (base+rva .. +VirtualSize) — the simplest defensible read; (b) D34 candidate enumeration for the USER projection = the one resolved (picked) `address_version` row per entity (the matched id is its `address_versions.id`); the multi-candidate gap-pass (DEV/Phase 5) enumerates an entity's full version-row set, but the entry-point shape returns the matched id either way; (c) a `data_slot` swept WITHOUT its anchor (the single-row sweep cannot thread a `CheckOrdered` anchor RVA) reads a DEFINED `cannot_check`/`anchor_unresolved`, never a faked verdict. **Row id:** extends the existing cap-84 (the survival-dispatch row) with the 3.2 + 3.3 sub-checks — same row, no new cap. |

## CAP-85: JS↔C++ cross-implementation survival agreement (the conformance pin, D27, step 3.4)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The CONFORMANCE PIN proving the C++ ENGINE static survival check and the JS BROWSER static check return the SAME verdict on the SAME DLL bytes for every in-scope kind (D27 — the engine is the authority, the browser its faithful mirror; the `version_resolver.py` test-of-record pattern, now applied to the per-kind survival check). The cross-implementation fixture (`data/refdata-extractor/python/seeds_shared/cross_impl_fixture.py`) is the SINGLE source-of-truth — it declares each `(kcdx_id, kind) → on-disk byte-slice(s) + pinned expected verdict` (Unchanged / Changed / Ambiguous / CannotCheck), derived from `fingerprint-per-kind.md` (NOT from any checker's output — the GROUND TRUTH every checker must reproduce). The fixture is emitted to a JSON contract (the Python source-of-truth → JSON → embedded in the engine via the GENERATED `src/survival_agreement_fixture.h`). For each fixture row's slice, the engine self-test: builds the survival `Payload`, PLANTS the slice's raw bytes in a SYNTHETIC PE at the right section/rva (the C++ analogue of the JS `makeAnchorPE`/`makeFakePE` planting — the engine's static checks scan PE SECTIONS, the fixture slices are just the kind's bytes), runs the REAL engine static check via the buffer-injection seam `survival::SurvivalCheckOnBuffer` (the IDENTICAL per-kind dispatch the production `SurvivalCheck` runs — NO new check logic), and asserts `engine_verdict == the fixture's pinned verdict` (== the Python reference == the JS browser checker). The agreement is over the ON-DISK version-applicability checks ONLY (D27); the loaded-image reachability check is engine-only (cap-84 sub-checks 7-9), OUTSIDE this pin. |
| Channels | Engine self-test (`src/survival_agreement_selftest.cpp`) — no plugin DLL, like cap-60 / cap-83 / cap-84. `survival::SurvivalCheckOnBuffer` is an engine-internal symbol (not author surface), so it self-reports from engine code via `kcdx::test::ReportResult`; a manifest-only stub plugin (`cap-85-survival-agreement/`) holds the row name via `test_names`. Single-surface (engine-internal). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/survival.{h,cpp}` add the **buffer-injection seam** `SurvivalCheckOnBuffer(payload, rva, fileBuffer)` (runs the existing per-kind dispatch over a caller-supplied PE buffer; the per-kind check logic is REUSED unchanged via a refactor extracting `PreReadDecision` + `CheckKindOverBuffer` + `CheckFunctionOnBuffer` — the production `SurvivalCheck` + the seam share the same core, only the byte SOURCE differs). The fixture JSON-export machinery lives in `cross_impl_fixture.py` (`fixture_to_json_obj`/`dump_fixture_json`/`fixture_header_text`/`dump_fixture_header`, additive); the committed contract is `data/refdata-extractor/tests/cross_impl_fixture.json` + the GENERATED `src/survival_agreement_fixture.h` (the engine-embedded JSON). Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-85-survival-agreement/`](cap-85-survival-agreement/) — manifest-only STUB (no plugin.lua, no DLL — like cap-84), holds `test_names = ["cap-85-survival-agreement"]`. Engine reports via `src/survival_agreement_selftest.cpp`, `RunSelfTestOnce()` one-shot from the per-tick self-report block right after cap-84. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`, HARD pass/fail (NOT degraded — synthetic PEs + an embedded fixture are self-contained, no WHGame.dll / DB dependency). One row, **cap-85-survival-agreement**: for every fixture slice of the algorithm-identical kinds — **function** (BLAKE3 body hash over a planted `.text` span; ALSO the cross-impl BLAKE3 hash agreement — the engine's vendored BLAKE3 must reproduce the pinned `content_hash`), **callsite** (AOB re-match over a planted `.text` block: unique→Unchanged / zero→Changed / multi→Ambiguous), **vtable_base** (N-qword table-shape over a planted `.data` table whose qwords rebase to `.text`-window pointers), **vtable_index** (CannotCheck, no PE) — the engine verdict via `SurvivalCheckOnBuffer` == the fixture's pinned verdict. `string_anchor` / `instruction_anchor` / `data_slot` are SURFACED (the engine static check is a SUPERSET of the cross-impl-agreed model over the fixture bytes — see Notes; NOT asserted this step). No player input — runs on the first suite tick. |
| Last result | (pending launch) — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-85-survival-agreement` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | The engine static verdict == the fixture's pinned verdict for EVERY asserted slice/kind. **FAILS if** the engine verdict diverges from the fixture's pinned verdict for ANY kind/slice (a real 3.1/3.2 engine bug the conformance test caught — the FAIL names the kind + both verdicts), **or** the embedded fixture JSON does not parse (a malformed/stale GENERATED header), **or** the pin did not cover every agreeing kind (a vacuous pass — AP15). Not a tautology (AP15): each assertion reads the ACTUAL engine check over the planted bytes and compares to a fixture verdict that is GROUND TRUTH (`fingerprint-per-kind.md`, not a checker's output) — the fixture is NEVER adjusted to match a wrong engine output, and the assertion is NEVER weakened (a divergence is surfaced as a real bug). |
| Notes | **The buffer-injection seam (a thin production headless-testability seam, surfaced):** the production `SurvivalCheck`/`CheckOrdered` hardcode reading WHGame.dll's backing file (no buffer-injection point), so driving the engine's REAL static check over fixture bytes needs a seam — `SurvivalCheckOnBuffer` runs the IDENTICAL per-kind dispatch over a caller buffer (`headless-testable.md` — build the seam, the primitive becomes a thin shell over tested logic). It is NOT new check logic: the refactor extracts the shared core (`PreReadDecision` + `CheckKindOverBuffer` + `CheckFunctionOnBuffer`) that BOTH the production path and the seam call; the production function path still routes through the legacy entry (byte-identical verdict — cap-84 sub-check 1 preserved). **The synthetic-PE helper (test-harness, not production):** the C++ analogue of `makeFakePE.ts` — a minimal PE32+ (ImageBase 0x180000000) with sections at chosen RVAs the `pe::OnDisk*` accessors parse; a `.text`/`.data` section's bytes are the planted slice. **SURFACED — the engine static check is a SUPERSET of the cross-impl-agreed (Python/JS) model for 3 kinds (a design fork, NOT silently resolved):** `string_anchor` — the engine ALSO runs the `expect_unique` `.text` LEA-xref check; the agreed model (`survival_checker.py`) is pure `.rdata` presence with the xref DOCUMENTED as deferred. `instruction_anchor` — the engine re-runs the FULL string→LEA resolver chain (needs a `.rdata` anchor string + a unique LEA in the PE); the agreed model is the forward shape+`disp32`-follow primitive (the fixture datum carries no anchor string). `data_slot` — the engine checks "the derivation lands in `.data`" + needs the `CheckOrdered` anchor-RVA threading; the agreed model checks "the `disp32`-follow reaches `expected_slot_rva`". Over the fixture's bytes these algorithms genuinely DIVERGE — so these 3 kinds are pinned only on the JS↔Python axis (the fixture rows are PRESENT in the JSON contract), and the engine↔browser pin for them is an OPEN design question (engine-fix-to-the-agreed-model vs scope-the-pin vs a richer fixture-PE). The pin asserts the 4 algorithm-identical kinds with a HARD assertion; the 3 superset kinds are surfaced, never masked (AP15). **Impact:** `survival.{h,cpp}` gain the seam + the shared-core refactor (no check-logic change, no behavior change to existing callers); `cross_impl_fixture.py` gains the additive JSON/header emitter; NEW TU `survival_agreement_selftest.{h,cpp}` (carries a compact dependency-free JSON reader + the synthetic-PE helper) + CMakeLists row + `hooks.cpp` wire (after cap-84); the JS side is UNTOUCHED (it already pins); the hook-backend files NOT touched. |

## CAP-86: `kcdx.locator.*` value namespace + the `:resolve(module, target)` introspection accessor (§9.3 step 1)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The `kcdx.locator.*` Lua value namespace (`src/lua_bind_locator.cpp`) — each locator form (`function_entry` / `function_exit` / `first_call_to` / `last_call_to` / `call_to` / `first_return` / `last_return` / `return_value` / `references_string` / `first_read_of_cvar` / `matching{…}` / `matching_pattern`) returns a locator VALUE (a userdata carrying a `StatementLocator` descriptor). The settled-fork `:resolve(module, target)` method on the value resolves the locator against a named curated function and returns `{ found, statement_idx, kind, byte_range_len, callee, string_ref, captures }` (or `{ found=false, reason }`). The accessor CALLS `refdb::ResolveStatementByName` — it does NOT reimplement resolution. The AUTHOR-SURFACE peer of cap-83 (which tests the engine `refdb` machinery from C++); cap-86 tests the Lua binding + the userdata + the `:resolve` seam. |

Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-86-locator-resolve/`](cap-86-locator-resolve/) — pure-Lua (no DLL — like cap-30), `kcdx.toml` + `plugin.lua`, holds `test_names = [12 cap-86-* rows]`. Resolves + reports at `kcdx.on("ready")` (refdb is open by then). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Twelve rows, one per locator family, each `:resolve("WHGame.dll", "SaveGame")` against ground truth measured from the curated DB (SaveGame, 59 statements): **cap-86-function-entry**→idx 0 (kind "assign", brl 3); **cap-86-function-exit**→idx 58 (kind "return", brl 30); **cap-86-first-call-to**(`FUN_1804d455c`)→idx 8 (callee, brl 5); **cap-86-last-call-to**(`FUN_1804d455c`)→idx 53 (brl 48) — first ≠ last; **cap-86-call-to**: `call_to(FUN_1805c38c8)` (UNIQUE)→idx 20 AND `call_to(FUN_1804d455c)` (called twice)→found=false reason `call_to_ambiguous`; **cap-86-first-return**→idx 13; **cap-86-last-return**→idx 58; **cap-86-return-value**(`cVar2`)→idx 55; **cap-86-references-string**(`" ignoring delay"`)→idx 1 (kind "assign", brl 7) — the first statement whose `string_ref` equals the arg; **cap-86-first-read-of-cvar**(`"Quick-saving"`)→idx 5 (kind "assign", brl 7) — a DISTINCT ground-truth `string_ref` value from references_string, proving the resolver reads the supplied name (the cvar name shares the `string_ref` column); **cap-86-matching**: `matching{}`→idx 0 AND `matching{kind="call"}`→idx 8; **cap-86-matching-pattern**(`48 8B C1`)→found=false reason `matching_pattern_not_statement_locator` (the labeled expert AOB hatch is NOT a statement-metadata locator). No player input — runs at ready. |
| Last result | (pending launch) — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-86-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | Each locator family's value `:resolve`s to the right statement against SaveGame. **FAILS if** a locator resolves to the WRONG `statement_idx` / `kind` / `byte_range_len` / `callee`, **or** `references_string` / `first_read_of_cvar` resolve to a statement other than their distinct ground-truth idx (1 vs 5) — or fail when the string IS present, **or** `call_to` on a doubly-called function does NOT reject with `call_to_ambiguous` (a duplicate call must error, not silently pick one), **or** `matching_pattern` resolves to ANY statement instead of rejecting with `matching_pattern_not_statement_locator`, **or** the `kcdx.locator` namespace is not registered. Each row reads the ACTUAL `:resolve` output, never a value it set (not a tautology — AP15). |
| Notes | **Author-surface vs engine machinery (the cap-83 split):** cap-83 tests `refdb::ResolveStatement*` from a C++ self-test (engine-internal); cap-86 tests the `kcdx.locator.*` Lua BINDING + the `:resolve` accessor (author surface) — non-duplicative coverage of the two layers. **Graceful-degraded (AP14):** the curated statement tables ship in the reference DB; if SaveGame does not resolve or carries no statements (a pre-deploy state where the deployed projection lacks the statement tables), the affected rows report a clear **DEGRADED PASS** (a deploy-state observation), never a hard FAIL or a crash — the `matching_pattern` reject row is NOT degraded-gated (the reject is the contract before any statement lookup). The degraded set is `db_not_loaded` + `function_no_statements` ONLY: `name_unknown` is deliberately NOT degraded (SaveGame resolves BY NAME, so a `name_unknown` is a real rename/renumber regression that must go RED, never a silent green — AP15). **Fixture currency:** SaveGame is referenced BY NAME (no hardcoded `address_id` — the curated set was renumbered, ids resolve by name). **Ground truth verified** against `data/reference.sqlite` (SaveGame av_id 304855; idx 0/58/8/53/20/13/58/55/1/5; `call_to_ambiguous` for `FUN_1804d455c` called at idx 8 + 53; `string_ref` stmts at idx 1 " ignoring delay" / 3 " immediately" / 5 "Quick-saving" / 6 "Saving") — expected values are the DB's, never adjusted to a resolution result. |

## CAP-87: `kcdx.op.*` static-bytes op value namespace + the `:emit_for(kind, byte_range_len)` introspection accessor (§9.3 step 2)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The `kcdx.op.*` Lua value namespace (`src/lua_bind_op.cpp`) — each op form (`replace_with_return`/`return_const` · `replace_return_value` · `replace_with_noop`/`noop` · `skip_call_void` · `skip_call_return_value` · `replace_call_target` · `always_take_branch` · `never_take_branch` · `invert_branch_condition` · `replace_assignment_value` · `replace_compare_constant`) returns an op VALUE (a userdata carrying an op descriptor). The settled-fork `:emit_for(kind, byte_range_len)` method on the value returns the emitted bytes for a statement-bytes-INDEPENDENT op (`{ kind_ok, deferred=false, bytes, fit }`), a deferral for a statement-bytes-DEPENDENT op (`{ kind_ok, deferred=true }`, bytes nil — needs the apply-time statement bytes), or a kind-mismatch teaching error (`{ kind_ok=false, reason }`). The sibling of cap-86 (`kcdx.locator.*`): a locator says WHERE, an op says WHAT static change. The same-size-vs-trampoline FIT pick is the STATEMENT VERB's at apply time (step 5); this step builds the op values + their byte-emit + the kind check. |

Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). |
