Builds op values + asserts `:emit_for` against SYNTHETIC kind+range inputs at `kcdx.on("ready")`. DB-INDEPENDENT — no reference-DB resolve, no `address_id`, no fixture (the op-emit is checkable without resolving any function). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Nine rows: **cap-87-noop-emit** `replace_with_noop:emit_for("call",5)`→`[90 90 90 90 90]`; **cap-87-skip-call-void-emit** `skip_call_void:emit_for("call",5)`→`[90×5]`; **cap-87-never-take-branch-emit** `never_take_branch:emit_for("branch",2)`→`[90 90]`; **cap-87-replace-with-return-zero** `replace_with_return(0):emit_for("return",5)`→`[31 C0 C3 90 90]` (xor eax,eax ; ret ; pad); **cap-87-replace-with-return-nonzero** `replace_with_return(110):emit_for("return",8)`→`[B8 6E 00 00 00 C3 90 90]` (mov eax,0x6E ; ret ; pad, little-endian imm32); **cap-87-replace-return-value-emit** `replace_return_value(1):emit_for("return",4)`→`[B8 01 00 00 00]` (mov eax,1, NO ret) fit=`trampoline` (5 bytes > the 4-byte range); **cap-87-skip-call-return-value-emit** `skip_call_return_value(0):emit_for("call",5)`→`[31 C0 90 90 90]`; **cap-87-deferred-ops** the 5 statement-bytes-dependent ops (`always_take_branch` / `invert_branch_condition` / `replace_call_target` / `replace_assignment_value` / `replace_compare_constant`) all report `kind_ok=true, deferred=true, bytes=nil`; **cap-87-kind-mismatch-reject** `always_take_branch:emit_for("call",5)`→`kind_ok=false` and the `reason` text names BOTH the actual kind (`call`) and the required kind (`branch`). No player input — runs at ready. |
| Last result | (pending launch) — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-87-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | Each op family emits the expected bytes (determinate ops) or defers (statement-bytes-dependent ops), and a kind-mismatch is rejected with a teaching error. **FAILS if** a determinate op's emitted bytes differ from the verified expectation (wrong opcode / wrong-endian imm32 / wrong value / wrong NOP-pad / a ret where there should be none), **or** a determinate op reports `deferred=true`, **or** an over-range determinate emit reports `fit="same_size"` instead of `"trampoline"`, **or** a statement-bytes-dependent op returns FABRICATED bytes instead of `deferred=true`+`bytes=nil` (guessing a branch displacement / call target / operand encoding is an AP14 silent defect), **or** the kind-mismatch does NOT reject (`kind_ok ~= false`), **or** the reject `reason` does not name the actual kind it was handed (`call`) — a tautology that always names the same fixed kind would not catch a wrong actual-kind report (AP15), **or** the `kcdx.op` namespace is not registered. Each row reads the ACTUAL `:emit_for` output, never a value it set. |
| Notes | **Op-value vs apply-time (the locator split, mirrored):** the op value carries the op descriptor + the byte-emit; the same-size-vs-trampoline pick reads the resolved statement's `byte_range_len` at apply time in the STATEMENT VERB (step 5), not here. cap-87 tests the op values + their `:emit_for` self-check seam; the apply-time install + the real-statement byte-emit are step 5's coverage. **Why two emit classes:** five ops (`always_take_branch` / `invert_branch_condition` / `replace_call_target` / `replace_assignment_value` / `replace_compare_constant`) cannot emit final bytes from kind+range alone — they need the apply-time statement's original bytes (a Jcc displacement/opcode, the call site, the operand encoding) or a resolved target address. `:emit_for` reports `deferred=true`, `bytes=nil` for them; the test asserts the deferral + the kind check, NEVER a fabricated byte (a wrong byte emit is an AP14-class silent defect — `results-driven.md`). The determinate ops (`replace_with_noop` / `skip_call_void` / `never_take_branch` / the return/value family) emit a statement-bytes-INDEPENDENT sequence asserted EXACTLY. **No fixture / DB dependency:** the rows feed synthetic kind+range pairs (no function resolved), so there is no `address_id`, no fixture-currency concern, and no graceful-degraded path. **Introspection accessor (settled fork):** `:emit_for(kind, byte_range_len)` mirrors cap-86's `:resolve(module, target)` — the seam an op value self-verifies through with no consuming verb; captured in the commit body per the decision-capture discipline. |

## CAP-88: `kcdx.functions.*` reference namespace + `kcdx.dll.declare` (§9.3 step 3a)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | The `kcdx.functions.*` function-reference value namespace + the `kcdx.dll.declare(plugin_namespace, function_map)` verb (`src/lua_bind_functions.cpp`). A `kcdx.functions.X.Y` access returns a function-reference VALUE (a userdata) the later hook (step 4) / statement (step 5) verbs accept as arg-1. TWO structurally-disjoint populations: **game-DLL functions** (dot-free stem, sourced from the reference DB — `kcdx.functions.WHGame.SaveGame`, plus the stable-id accessor `kcdx.functions.by_id[N]`, game-only) and **plugin-DLL functions** (dotted `<author>.<plugin>` stem, bracket-indexed, sourced from the author via `kcdx.dll.declare` — `kcdx.functions["a.b"].Fn`). The settled-fork `:resolve()` accessor (mirroring cap-86 `:resolve` / cap-87 `:emit_for`) returns `{ found, is_game, stem, name, signature, has_address, address }`. **3a is the deterministic half** — game functions enter via the reference DB; plugin functions enter via `kcdx.dll.declare` (the signature, copied from the author's source — the one irreducible thing a callback hook needs). The PDB auto-load + C-export ADDRESS path for plugin functions is step 3b (a declared plugin reference carries its signature with `has_address=false` here — not a failure). |
| Channels | Pure-Lua plugin (`cap-88-plugin-fn-declare/plugin.lua`) — drives the author surface from Lua and self-reports via `kcdx.test.report`. C++ parity (`kcdxFunctionsInterface` / `kcdxDllInterface`) is a marked NYI mirror (`docs/cpp/functions.md` + `docs/cpp/dll.md`); the built C++ mirror is a later step. |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/lua_bind_functions.cpp` registers the `kcdx.functions` namespace (a `__index`-dispatching table: no-dot stem → game proxy resolving by name via `refdb::ResolveByName`; dotted stem → plugin proxy reading the `kcdx.dll.declare` store; `by_id` → game-only id proxy via `refdb::ResolveById`) + the `kcdx.dll` domain (`declare`) + the function-reference-value metatable (`:resolve`/`__gc`); wired into `src/lua_bind.cpp::RegisterKcdxTable` after `kcdx.op`. Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-88-plugin-fn-declare/`](cap-88-plugin-fn-declare/) — pure-Lua (no DLL — like cap-86/87), `kcdx.toml` + `plugin.lua`, holds `test_names = [3 cap-88-* rows]`. Declares + resolves + reports at `kcdx.on("ready")` (refdb is open by then). |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Three rows: **cap-88-declare-populates-namespace** — `kcdx.dll.declare("a.test", { DeclaredFn = { signature = "bool (ptr self)" } })` returns true AND `kcdx.functions["a.test"].DeclaredFn:resolve()` reports `found=true is_game=false signature="bool (ptr self)"` (the author-declared ABI; DB-INDEPENDENT, no resolve); **cap-88-game-fn-resolves** — `kcdx.functions.WHGame.SaveGame:resolve()` reports `found=true is_game=true has_address=true` with a non-zero `address` (resolved BY NAME against the reference DB; verified signature carried); **cap-88-by-id-resolves** — `kcdx.functions.by_id[131]:resolve()` (CCryPak_FOpen, a CURRENT curated id in the contiguous 1-157 scheme) reports `found=true is_game=true has_address=true`. No player input — runs at ready. |
| Last result | (pending launch) — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-88-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | A declared plugin function resolves to a reference carrying its author-declared signature; a game-DLL function resolves by name + by id to a non-zero address. **FAILS if** `kcdx.dll.declare` does NOT populate `kcdx.functions["a.test"].DeclaredFn` (found=false / not_declared), **or** the resolved declared reference lacks the `"bool (ptr self)"` signature (the reference lost it), **or** the declared reference is mis-classified as a game reference (`is_game ~= false`), **or** `kcdx.functions.WHGame.SaveGame` does not resolve (found=false reason `name_unknown` — a real rename/renumber regression, NOT degraded), **or** SaveGame / `by_id[131]` resolve with no address (`has_address ~= true`), **or** a game stem reference reports `is_game ~= true`, **or** the `kcdx.functions` / `kcdx.dll` namespaces are not registered. Each row reads the ACTUAL `:resolve` output, never a value it set (not a tautology — AP15). |
| Notes | **3a-only scope:** the cross-plugin HOOK-and-FIRE half (plugin B hooks A's declared fn) rides STEP 4 (the reference-accepting hook does not exist yet) — cap-88 proves the declaration + resolution machinery only. The PDB auto-load + C-export address path for plugin functions is step 3b (gated behind a separate probe); a declared plugin reference carries its signature here with `has_address=false`, which is correct, not a gap (a callback hook needs the signature; a static byte op needs the address — 3b adds the address). **Game-DLL population shape (the settled call):** a no-dot stem access returns a per-stem PROXY whose `__index` resolves a name on first access via `refdb::ResolveByName` (LAZY), the equivalent of "eager at startup" against the same contract (a name → address + ABI) without forcing a startup walk to group a flat name cache into per-stem subtables — refdb is keyed by canonical name and exposes no per-DLL-stem bulk enumerate. The signature for a game function IS available from refdb (`NameResolution.verified_signature`, the abi_walker dump), so a game reference carries address AND signature. **Fixture currency (AP1 / TD-0008 class):** SaveGame is referenced BY NAME (no hardcoded `address_id`); `by_id[131]` (CCryPak_FOpen) is a CURRENT curated id in the renumbered 1-157 scheme, NOT a retired id (1172/1124 are gone). **Graceful-degraded (AP14):** if the reference DB is not open (a pre-deploy state, reason `db_not_loaded`), the two game-resolve rows report a clear DEGRADED PASS (the namespace + `:resolve` are wired; the address check is skipped); `name_unknown` is deliberately NOT degraded (a real regression that must go RED). The declare row is DB-INDEPENDENT (no degraded path). **Introspection accessor (settled fork):** `:resolve()` mirrors cap-86 `:resolve(module, target)` / cap-87 `:emit_for(kind, byte_range_len)` — the seam a reference value self-verifies through with no consuming verb. |

## CAP-90: PDB auto-load of plugin-DLL internal-function addresses (§9.3 step 3b)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| What | PDB auto-load (`src/plugin_pdb.{cpp,h}`): at C++ plugin load, the engine parses the plugin DLL's sidecar `/DEBUG:FULL` `.pdb` via DbgHelp (`SymInitialize` + `SymLoadModuleEx` + `SymEnumSymbols`) and populates the plugin's OWN internal (NON-exported) FUNCTION addresses into `kcdx.functions["<author>.<plugin>"].*` — source #2 of the three plugin-function address sources, the ADDITIVE half that fills the `has_address` that step 3a's declare path leaves unset. The declare (signature) path is untouched: a function can have a declared signature (callback-hookable, no address yet), a PDB address (static-op-able, no signature), or both. The CRT/compiler-plumbing filter is **keep-by-default, positive-ID-to-drop**: after the in-range (`[base, base+imageSize)`) + `SymTagFunction` gates, a function is KEPT unless POSITIVELY identified as runtime plumbing — (a) a source line under a CRT/compiler build path (markers: `vctools\crt`, `ucrt`, `vcruntime`, `vccrt`, `vcstartup`, `minkernel\crts`), OR (b) for a function the PDB gives NO source line, a positive CRT/STL NAME match (`LooksLikeCrtName`: `std::`/`type_info`/`__acrt_`/`__crt_`/`_CRT`/`__scrt_`/`operator new`+`delete`/`_purecall`/`__std_`/`_Xlength_error`/`_Xout_of_range`/a `$` funclet, …). A function matching NEITHER — including one with no source line and a plain name — is KEPT, because the hard requirement is that an author's OWN function is NEVER dropped on a guess. There is no path that drops a function for an unrecognized reason. Every reject emits a per-function `LOG_DEBUG_KV("PDB","filtered", name, reason="crt_source"|"crt_name")` so an author can see (under dev mode) exactly what was dropped and why. Input-validation caps bound the work (`kMaxFunctions`, name validation, an out-of-range address rejected). |
| Channels | One plugin, two entrypoints: a C++ DLL (`cap-90.cpp` — the non-exported internal + the `/DEBUG:FULL` PDB) + a pure-Lua self-check (`plugin.lua` — reads the address back via `:resolve`, reports via `kcdx.test.report`). C++ parity for the surface (`kcdxFunctionsInterface`) is the marked NYI mirror (`docs/cpp/functions.md`); 3b adds no new C++ author API (the address source is engine-internal, driven at plugin load). |
| Engine status | LIVE — `src/plugin_pdb.cpp` runs at the C++ Load wave (`src/plugin_loader.cpp`, after `p.loaded = ok`, with `p.module` + `p.filePath` in hand) for every DLL-bearing plugin; records via `kcdx::lua_bind_functions::RecordPluginAddress` into a mutex-guarded address store `ResolveRef` reads. Three graceful-fallback cases each log a teaching line (no PDB / load-or-GUID mismatch / FASTLINK-stub). Build/matrix UNCONFIRMED by the implementing agent (the user runs `pwsh ./build.ps1` + the launch; the agent reads the log). |
| Test plugin | [`cap-90-pdb-autoload/`](cap-90-pdb-autoload/) — `kcdx.toml` (`[entrypoints] dll = "cap-90.dll"` + `lua = "plugin.lua"`) + `cap-90.cpp` (its own CMakeLists pins `/DEBUG:FULL`) + `plugin.lua`, holds `test_names = [3 cap-90-* rows]`. The DLL's `kcdxPlugin_Load` fires at load (PDB auto-load runs there); the Lua rows resolve + report at `kcdx.on("ready")`. The cap-90 fixture DLL is unchanged for this hardening — only `plugin.lua` + `kcdx.toml` add the third row. |
| Auto-pass check | `boot-only`. Three rows. **cap-90-pdb-internal-address** — `kcdx.functions["ts.cap_90_pdb_autoload"].cap90_internal_target:resolve()` reports `found=true is_game=false has_address=true` with a non-zero `address`. The internal `cap90_internal_target` is non-exported by construction (verify: `dumpbin /EXPORTS cap-90.dll` lists only `kcdxPlugin_Load`), so its address can ONLY have come from the PDB auto-load path (the KEEP side — an author's own source-line function). **cap-90-crt-filtered** — `kcdx.functions["ts.cap_90_pdb_autoload"]["operator delete"]:resolve()` reports `found=false`: the C-runtime `operator delete` the linker pulls into the DLL is REJECTED by the positive-ID filter (a CRT source path OR the exact-name match), so it never populates the namespace. **cap-90-stl-no-source-filtered** — `kcdx.functions["ts.cap_90_pdb_autoload"]["bad_exception"]:resolve()` reports `found=false`: `std::bad_exception::bad_exception` (bare leaf `bad_exception`) has NO source line, so it is rejected ONLY by the positive CRT-NAME branch (it starts with `std::`) — proving the name check catches no-source plumbing the keep-by-default filter would otherwise keep. No player input — all three run at ready. |
| Last result | (pending launch) — confirmed at the next launch (agent reads `suite: X/Y` + any `FAIL cap-90-*` from `kcdx-dev.log`). |
| Falsifiable claim / FAIL condition | **cap-90-pdb-internal-address:** a plugin's non-exported internal function resolves its address from the plugin's own shipped `/DEBUG:FULL` PDB. FAILS if `:resolve()` reports `has_address ~= true` / a nil-or-zero `address` (PDB auto-load did not run, the `/DEBUG:FULL` PDB was absent/mismatched, OR — the specific regression this row guards — a present FULL PDB silently degraded to FASTLINK and the fallback dropped the internals), **or** `found ~= true` (`not_declared` — the internal never populated), **or** the internal is mis-classified `is_game ~= false`, **or** the namespace is not registered. **cap-90-crt-filtered:** the SOURCE-PATH reject — a CRT function with a CRT build-tree source line is dropped. FAILS if the CRT `operator delete` RESOLVES (`found=true`) under the plugin namespace, meaning the positive-ID filter did not run or is too loose. **cap-90-stl-no-source-filtered:** the NAME reject — a CRT/STL function with NO source line is dropped by its name pattern. FAILS if `std::bad_exception::bad_exception` (leaf `bad_exception`) RESOLVES (`found=true`), meaning the no-source NAME-check branch did not run (the keep-by-default filter would then keep it) or its pattern list omits `std::`. All three rows read the ACTUAL `:resolve` output (not a tautology); there is no degraded path (the plugin's own namespace populated by its own PDB — no reference-DB dependency). **Keep-by-default safety — the design guarantee, not directly tested here:** a no-source function whose name matches NO CRT pattern is KEPT, so an author's own function is never dropped on a guess. The fixture cannot exercise this keep-side directly (its own author function `cap90_internal_target` HAS a source line, so it takes the source-line KEEP path, not the no-source one); the three rows prove the two REJECT sides (CRT source path + CRT name) and the source-line KEEP side, and the keep-by-default-for-an-unknown-no-source-function is the implementation guarantee the inverted filter encodes. |
| Notes | **The load-bearing build constraint (proven by a falsify-then-confirm probe pair):** internal auto-load works ONLY with a `/DEBUG:FULL` (self-contained) PDB; a FASTLINK PDB (the VS2017+ default) is a build-machine-OBJ-indexing stub that carries NO private symbols when deployed (`SymLoadModuleEx` SUCCEEDS, the enumerate yields zero of the plugin's own functions). The cap-90 CMakeLists pins `/DEBUG:FULL` + strips any inherited FASTLINK so the fixture ships the right kind. **The three fallback cases each teach (logging.md):** no `.pdb` beside the DLL → INFO "no PDB … using exports + declared"; `.pdb` present but load/GUID-age mismatch → WARN "PDB … doesn't match its DLL"; `.pdb` loads but ZERO in-range functions (the FASTLINK/stub case the probe specifically found) → WARN "plugin ships a FASTLINK PDB; rebuild with /DEBUG:FULL …" — NOT lumped into the generic no-PDB line. **Undeclared-internal resolution:** an internal the PDB found but the author never declared resolves ADDRESS-ONLY (`signature=""`, `has_address=true`) — a static op works; a callback hook on it reports "signature needed, declare it" (compiled C++ carries no runtime-queryable ABI). **The seam:** `plugin_pdb` writes into an address store `lua_bind_functions` owns (matching the 3a `g_declared` idiom — a mutex-guarded in-memory map), SEPARATE from the signature store so the two facts are orthogonal; `ResolveRef` reads both and the reference is found if EITHER is present. **The author-only filter (keep-by-default, positive-ID-to-drop):** the enumerate also yields the CRT/compiler-internal functions the linker pulls into every plugin DLL (`operator delete`, `std::bad_exception`, `_set_new_handler`, `__crt_seh_guarded_call`, …) — runtime plumbing nobody hooks. The hardened filter DEFAULTS TO KEEP and drops a function ONLY when it is POSITIVELY identified as CRT plumbing, because the hard requirement is that an author's OWN function is NEVER dropped on a guess: (a) a source line under a CRT/compiler build path (`vctools\crt`, `ucrt`, `vcruntime`, `vccrt`, `vcstartup`, `minkernel\crts`) → reject (`crt_source`); (b) for a function the PDB gives NO source line, a positive CRT/STL NAME match (`LooksLikeCrtName`: `std::`/`type_info`/`__acrt_`/`__crt_`/`_CRT`/`__scrt_`/`operator new`+`delete`/`_purecall`/`__std_`/`_Xlength_error`/`_Xout_of_range`/a `$` funclet) → reject (`crt_name`); (c) ANY function matching neither — including one with NO source line and a plain name — is KEPT (counted `kept_no_source` in the success INFO line). The earlier `no_source`-as-blanket-reject bucket is GONE — there is no path that drops a function for an unrecognized reason. Every reject emits one `LOG_DEBUG_KV("PDB","filtered", namespace, name, reason)` (DEBUG, off in production, on under dev mode — a cold one-shot plugin-load path, so per-function debug lines are fine, `logging.md`) so an author who suspects a wrongly-filtered function can turn on dev mode and SEE it + why. So `kcdx.functions["<author>.<plugin>"]` carries the author's own code, and the CRT plumbing nobody hooks is dropped only when positively identified. **Input-validation (the plugin DLL + PDB are an external-authored on-disk artifact):** the enumerate caps recorded functions at `kMaxFunctions` (a malformed/huge PDB cannot OOM the map), validates symbol names (no interior NUL, under a length cap) before keying, and the in-range `[base, base+imageSize)` + `SymTagFunction` filter rejects everything not a function in THIS module's own image; the PDB-supplied source file is lowercased + substring-scanned only (never used as a real path). |

# Section 2.5: Asset-system coverage matrix (the permanent regression net's index)

The asset-replacement system's six acceptance cases (asset design §3 US-1/US-3/US-4/US-7 + §4.4 + §7) mapped to the rows that cover each — the single scannable index of the permanent asset-system regression net. Four cases were already covered by earlier asset-system steps' same-change tests; step 10 added the two genuine gaps (the served-`.lua` EXECUTE proof + the stock-pak US-7 fall-through). Rows are defined in full above (Section 1) / below (Section 2 cross-mod) and in the live roll-up table — this section cites them, it does not duplicate them.

| # | Case (asset design) | Covering row(s) | Mode | Falsifiable claim (what makes it FAIL) | Last result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **US-1** — replace a vanilla asset, no code (declarative sidecar) | `CAP-73-declared-applies` | boot-only | the declared overlay is NOT keyed into the map by its declared target (`overlay_entry` line absent) → FAIL | ✅ PASS (2026-06-04) |
| 2 | **add-new** (loose lane, HOOK 2 own-`FILE*`) — a declared loose `.lua`/`.xml` overlay is SERVED end-to-end | `CAP-73-handle-consumed-serve` | in-game | the engine opens the keyed `.lua` and HOOK 2 does NOT serve its own `FILE*` (`map=HIT` / `probe_fopen_hc_served` absent) → FAIL | ✅ PASS (LIVE 2026-06-04) |
| 3 | **serve-AND-EXECUTE** — a served handle-consumed `.lua` actually RUNS (the serve was proven earlier — `CAP-73-handle-consumed-serve` PASS; this closes the EXECUTE half) | `CAP-78-serve-execute` (boot `CAP-78-keyed`) — re-vehicle; supersedes the falsified `CAP-77-serve-execute` | in-game | `KCDX_KI6_OVERLAY_SERVED_AND_RAN` reaches the game log (kcdx's overlay of a loose mod-init `.lua` served by HOOK 2 ran) → PASS | **KI-0006 (PROBE A pending launch)** — cap-77's `sl_saveload.lua` was falsified `23-11-37`; the re-vehicle keys a loose `scripts/mods/<modid>.lua` (the engine runs it every boot, opens it via HOOK 2's FOpen lane). The serve mechanism is proven (CAP-73); only the EXECUTE leg is open. |
| 4 | **US-3** — reference another mod's asset (by name / by path) | `CAP-75-cross`, `CAP-75-getbyname-cross`, `CAP-74-resolves` | boot-only | the cross-plugin `kcdx.plugin.<a>.<p>.assets.*` navigation raises / returns nil / wrong file → FAIL | ✅ PASS |
| 5 | **US-4 + §4.4** — replace another mod's asset (chain) + the conflict winner/loser | `CAP-73-conflict`, `COMP-16-replace-code` | boot-only | two declarations of one target do NOT resolve by load order with the §4.4 conflict line / the resolvable cross-mod name does NOT resolve to A's serve-vpath → FAIL | ✅ PASS |
| 6 | **US-7 + §7** — a stock Nexus/Workshop pak resolves UNCHANGED through HOOK 1's MISS fall-through (id 153) + the engine's mount/stream lane | `COMP-17-stock-pak-fallthrough` | [manual] (boot) | the stock pak's in-pak Lua marker `[KCDX_PROBE]` reaches `kcd.log` → PASS | ✅ **PASS** (`23-11-37` — 30 `[KCDX_PROBE]` lines; the non-overlay pak resolved + ran unchanged) |

# Section 3: Real-world mod scenarios

Sanity-check the matrix by walking real mod ideas end-to-end. For
each, list the capability rows the mod needs and note if any are
DEFERRED.

## Scenario A: "Combat tweaks" (the outfit-swap case)

Small mod, single byte rewrite. Maps to **CAP-01** alone. Already
proven via CAP-01 test plugin.

## Scenario B: "Better hud" (UI addition with custom data)

Mod displays player stamina/stamina-regen in a custom HUD widget.
Needs: **CAP-19** (Scaleform injection — DEFERRED) + **CAP-06**
(read game state via dynamic_call to grab stamina values) +
**CAP-08** (subscribe to gameplay-tick message to refresh the
widget). Buildable when v0.2 ships Scaleform.

## Scenario C: "Persistent perk system" (new game mechanic)

Mod adds a perk that the player buys with XP. Perk persists
across saves. Needs: **CAP-10** (expose `kcdx.perks.buy()` to
pak Lua) + **CAP-12** (save/load perks state) + **CAP-13**
(console command to grant perks for testing) + **CAP-18** (pak resource override for the
perks XML).

## Scenario D: "Magic system" (full new system, SKSE-class)

Player can cast spells. Spells have effects, mana cost,
cooldowns. Needs: **CAP-10** (`kcdx.magic.cast`) + **CAP-12**
(save mana/known-spells — DEFERRED) + **CAP-13** (console
commands for testing — DEFERRED) + **CAP-08** (subscribe to
input events for hotkeys) + **CAP-18** (XML/Lua for spell
definitions) + **CAP-19** (UI for spell selection — DEFERRED).
Plus extension points: **CAP-06** for dependent mods to
read magic state, **CAP-10** for them to register new spells.

## Scenario E: "Workshop-distributable code injection"

Mod author wants to ship a single .pak that does some byte
rewriting (e.g., the outfit-swap). Needs to work via Steam
Workshop (which only accepts paks). Maps to **CAP-05** alone.
This is the novel kcdx capability — before kcdx, this was
impossible (pak Lua had no FFI).

## Scenario F: "Cross-plugin ecosystem" (the hard one)

Plugin A is a magic system. Plugin B adds new spells to A.
Plugin C adds a UI for spell selection that works with both
A's vanilla spells and B's additions. Needs: **CAP-16**
(dependencies — A then B then C) + **CAP-10** (each plugin's
Lua surface) + **CAP-06** (C reads A's registered spells) +
**COMP-05** (registration override behavior) + **COMP-06**
(dependency chain proven). Buildable today modulo the
registration-override warning ergonomics.

---

| COMP-16-replace-code | ✅ PASS | _crossmod_ | `boot-only`. Cross-mod RESOLUTION (US-4, design §5.3): publisher A (`comp-16-crossmod-publisher`) sidecar-publishes `belt` (a publish-and-replace, so its serve-vpath = the vanilla vpath it replaces); consumer B (`comp-16-crossmod-consumer-code`) does `replace("ts.comp_16_publisher_a.belt", "data/comp16_b.xml")` — the PACKED cross-mod form. The two-hop §5.3 resolution: (1) the packed name resolves to A's serve-vpath; (2) the runtime-overlay store is keyed by it. B's `replace` returns a loadable path (step 8b returned a teaching error here — step 8c resolves). FALSIFIABLE: `(nil, err)` for the resolvable published name (resolution did not land / A's publish not visible) OR a non-string/empty return → FAIL. Stresses the TWO-PASS build (A loads LAST at priority 90; `belt` is sidecar-published at build so B resolves it ordering-independently) (`comp-16-crossmod-publisher` + `comp-16-crossmod-consumer-code`) |
| COMP-16-serve-code | DEFERRED → Phase 11 | _crossmod_ | `in-game`. The cross-mod SERVE end-to-end: B's file serves where A's `belt` would, at A's serve-vpath, IN-GAME (US-4 — `source=runtime` naming B as winner). DEFERRED for the SAME reason as CAP-75-register-serve: B's `replace("ts.comp_16_publisher_a.belt", with)` runs at `plugin.lua` time (post-VM), so it cannot serve a boot-cached serve-vpath. The cross-mod RESOLUTION (the two-hop §5.3 name→serve-vpath→store-key) is LIVE and PASSES at boot (COMP-16-replace-code); only the in-game SERVE is Phase-11-gated (the DllMain Lua VM — `before-game-hooks.md` §6b — lets the consumer's replace run before the boot open). When Phase 11 lands, this row re-vehicles to a confirmed after-VM serve-vpath and PROBE B confirms the serve. FALSIFIABLE (Phase 11): the engine opens A's serve-vpath on a save-load and NO `source=runtime` line naming B appears → FAIL (`comp-16-crossmod-consumer-code`) |
| COMP-02 | ✅ PASS | _migration_ | `kcdx.bytes` patch + `kcdx.hook` detour on ONE function-entry VA (id 1006, offset −4); cross-engine coexist. `GetConflictReport(entry VA)` = 2 entries — `comp02_patch`(kind=Patch,applied) + `comp02_hook`(kind=Hook,applied). Patch engine + hook_chain coexist like legacy `[[patch]]`+`[[hook]]`. The probe launch caught a patch-after-hook apply-order bug (patch byte-verify aborted on the hook's `E9`); fixed by the ApplyZone kind rank (Bytes before Hook) — patch writes pristine bytes first, hook then detours the patched prologue (`comp-02-hook-on-patch`) |
| COMP-03 | ✅ LIVE | _ | hook-on-hook A + B; conflict report verified |
| PROBE-COMP-CRASH | ✅ LIVE | _ | conflict-report-crash regression guard |
| COMP-01, COMP-04, COMP-05, COMP-06, COMP-08 | DEFERRED-v0.2 | _ | conflict-matrix completeness lives in internal recon notes; v0.1 ships CAP-* coverage of the primary primitives |
| COMP-07 | OUT-OF-SCOPE | _ | pak + DLL cross-channel; not a v0.1 surface |
| CAP-90-pdb-internal-address | ⏳ PENDING | §9.3-3b | `boot-only`. PDB auto-load: a C++ plugin's non-exported internal `cap90_internal_target` resolves its address from the plugin's own shipped `/DEBUG:FULL` PDB — `kcdx.functions["ts.cap_90_pdb_autoload"].cap90_internal_target:resolve()` reports `has_address=true` + a non-zero address. FALSIFIABLE: `has_address ~= true` / nil-or-zero address (PDB auto-load did not run, no/mismatched PDB, OR a present FULL PDB silently degraded to FASTLINK) → FAIL (`cap-90-pdb-autoload`) |

---

# Section 5: Authoring a new test plugin

Loop for each row that needs a real test plugin:

1. Create the folder `test-plugins/<row-id>-<short-name>/` (e.g.
   `cap-05-paklua-runtime/`).
2. Drop in a `kcdx.toml` with `[kcdx] test_suite_only = true`.
3. If the test needs a DLL: add `CMakeLists.txt` + `.cpp` next to
   the TOML. In `kcdxPlugin_Load`, check `dev::IsEnabled()`,
   run the test logic, call `api->ReportTestResult(handle,
   "<row-id>", pass, "<one-sentence reason>")`.
4. If the test needs a pak: add a `pak/` subdir with `Data/*.pak`
   + `mod.manifest`. The pak's Lua script checks
   `kcdx.dev.is_enabled()`, runs the test, calls
   `kcdx.test.report("<row-id>", pass, "<reason>")`.
5. Install the plugin to the game, launch with dev mode on,
   verify the suite picks up the new test and reports it.
6. Update the row's Last result + Section 4 roll-up + commit.

Folder hygiene: don't pollute test-plugins/ with utility files
(build scripts, README per plugin, etc.). The plugin should be
the minimum viable thing that asserts the capability. If it
needs README text, put it inline in the plugin's kcdx.toml as a
comment block.
